```text
2026-10-13 / PROCESS-FINDING-1 / a stop order did not prevent a post-order amend (recorded as the owner directed)
What happened, stated plainly: the outgoing COLLECT-REMOTE-1 orchestrator session (`ses_ecf1aa13fffeeXDEKIs0m0lfU8`) was ordered to stop making edits, commits and pushes. It amended `ab58699` to `8b7e47b` at 23:27:19 local anyway. The handoff capture the directing session had taken referenced `ab58699`, a commit that no longer existed on the branch, and the running record had to be corrected in four places (slice hash, insertion count, the regression-power note, and an F1 status the amend had already partly fixed).
Why it was contained: the incoming orchestrator (`ses_ec9e8c9dcffeoJwrRq0LpdQ0UI`) audited the inherited state instead of trusting the packet, found the discrepancy by comparing the packet's snapshot to `git status`, `git log` and the reflog, and reconciled it; nothing inherited was discarded. That audit is the reason this stayed a records correction rather than a lost-review incident.
Lesson to carry: after ordering a writer to stop, do not capture its state until the stop is CONFIRMED, or capture again afterwards; a stop order is not itself confirmation that no further write occurred. The incoming session applied the lesson to itself: it added follow-up commits rather than amending an existing one, so a later reader is never handed a reference to a commit that no longer exists.
Next gate and blockers: none.
```

```text
2026-10-13/14 / REVIEW-UNDO-1 / authorized -> designed -> implemented -> verified -> reviewed -> review-fixed -> re-reviewed -> records
Owner authority reference: owner-authorised REVIEW-UNDO-1 packet (design pass and implementation for issues #46 and #47 inside the named seams; tests; independent review; records; one pull request; a later decision packet that put `internal/review/review.go` inside the allowed set and fixed the reset scope, the submitted-interval rule, and the mandatory confirmed listing). Not authorised: merging the pull request, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch, touching issues other than #46/#47 and new ones filed, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification.
Branch / HEAD / exact snapshot: `feat/review-undo-1` from `main` `2a49400`. Two commits: `11f7b03` (the slice, 10 files, +783/-50) and `ca9e525` (the review fixes, F1-F4/F6). Ten files: `internal/review/review.go`, `internal/run/review.go`, `internal/run/run.go`, `cmd/timesheet/main.go`, `cmd/timesheet/interactive.go`, three test files, and `.claude/skills/timesheet/{workflow.md,SKILL.md}`. No file outside the approved set was touched and no untracked file was left.
Defects, with evidence: #46 - `exclude` cleared a confirmed interval's `evidence_ids` but left it confirmed (`internal/review/review.go` `ExcludeEvidence`, kept a manual interval with cleared evidence), and `mark_empty` refused any day carrying a confirmed interval, so no decision could remove one and the owner had to rebuild 2026-10-07 by hand. #47 - no subcommand and no decision key cleared a day's review outcome; the owner's workaround was to move `~/.timesheet/records/workflow/2026-10-07.json` aside, which is outside every documented step.
Design verdict (lane @oracle `ses_ec9d08590ffecdUz5Ag805Jc2j`, read-only): the design was SOUND and the correct mechanism already existed, but it required adding two decision keys, a real removal primitive that existed in no layer, and `internal/review/review.go`, which was OUTSIDE the implementer's file set. That boundary was reported to the owner before any expansion, together with the alternative that stayed inside the set, and the owner chose the expansion.
Design answers to the five questions: (1) unsubmitted unconfirm REMOVES the interval and returns its evidence to the proposal as an unconfirmed block; it does not become excluded, because an exclusion is a different decision and reusing it would recreate the half-applied state. Evidence items stay in `CollectedEvidence`; classification and a typed description are confirmation attributes and are re-chosen on re-confirm; a manual span re-materialises as manual evidence; a split interval's fragments are removed together. (2) a SUBMITTED interval (one with a `RemoteMapping`) is BLOCKED with the specific reason `unconfirm_mapped_remote:<localID>` pointing at `trim`/`project`/`description`/`classification`, because a Gradion entry is editable but never removable (`PlanOpKind` has only `create`/`edit`), so allowing it locally would describe a state no operation could ever clear. (3) approval invalidation REUSES the existing mechanism: `unconfirm`/`reset` were added to `scheduleAffecting`, which drives `invalidateApprovalsForDate` -> `workflow.InvalidateApproval`, with `LocalFingerprint`/`ValidateApproval` as the backstop; no parallel mechanism was invented. (4) `reset` is a DECISION KEY returning the day to a clean review slate; it clears the review outcome and every unsubmitted confirmation and the other review decisions, retains evidence, mappings, plans, approvals (invalidated, never deleted), outcomes and unresolved history, never deletes the record file, and blocks on a submitted interval or an unresolved operation. (5) the surface is the two decision keys (reachable from `review --decisions` and the guided path), documented completely in both skill files, PLUS the mandatory `Runner.Confirmed` listing that prints a day's confirmed intervals with full ids on the scripted `review` path, because no supported command printed a confirmed interval's id before this slice and the feature would otherwise be unusable.
Owner decisions applied: (a) expand the file set to `internal/review/review.go` rather than create a second removal path in `internal/workflow`; (b) the submitted case BLOCKS and points at an edit; (c) `reset` clears the WHOLE review decision set, including exclusions, attendance and acknowledgements, so a reset is never a half-applied state under another name; (d) the `Confirmed` full-id listing is in scope and not optional.
Behavior change: `ReviewDecisions` gains `unconfirm` (`block_id`, `reason`) and `reset` (bool). `applyDecisions` applies reset, then unconfirm, then confirm, with a proposal recompute in between, so one decision set can remove a wrong interval and re-confirm its block in a single pass. `review.Unconfirm` removes a confirmed interval and every fragment sharing its non-empty `SplitGroupID`, refusing a mapped member and an unknown id. `review.ResetReview` clears the day's confirmed work and the whole review decision set. Both run inside the store's day session, so a refusal writes NOTHING.
Invariants held: an unconfirm or reset never leaves a half-applied state; retained history is never erased (evidence, mappings, plans, approvals, outcomes, unresolved history and the record file all survive a reset); the submitted case is deliberately BLOCKED rather than implemented, so no code path can orphan a mapping or create a silent remote replacement; an unconfirm or reset never leaves a valid approval pointing at a changed record; every refusal carries a specific reason (`unconfirm_mapped_remote:<id>`, `unconfirm_unknown_interval:<id>`, `reset_submitted_interval:<id>`, `reset_unresolved_submission`), never a generic failure. `internal/submit`'s plan/execute path and the exactly-once write path are untouched.
Regression tests, each demonstrated to fail before and pass after: unsubmitted unconfirm returns the block to the proposal without dropping evidence; a submitted-interval unconfirm blocks with the reason, the id and the edit remedy, writing nothing; a split-group unconfirm whose MAPPED SIBLING is not the requested id blocks and writes nothing; a reset clears the review decisions while retaining evidence, mappings, outcomes, plans and the invalidated approval row; a reset blocks on a submitted interval and on an `in_flight` outcome, writing nothing; approval invalidation for unconfirm and for reset, including the `mark_empty` combinations; guided/scripted parity for the removal; a mapped interval (and any fragment whose group is mapped) presented as non-removable; both fragments of a split group removed in one guided pass without `unconfirm_unknown_interval`, with the emptied day recorded honestly. Fail-before was demonstrated by temporarily reverting each fix and capturing the failing assertions, then restoring byte-exactly.
Independent review, round 1: fresh lane @oracle `ses_ec9822f03ffej4ot4a6qIlfBmq`, read-only, on `11f7b03`. Verdict CHANGES REQUIRED. It answered the four owner questions and raised six findings; it independently reproduced two of them with `go -overlay` probes against production code.
Review findings and dispositions: F1 (medium) FIXED - `mark_empty` combined with `unconfirm`/`reset` skipped eager approval invalidation because the `mark_empty` branch returned before the `scheduleAffecting` block; the invalidation now runs FIRST. F2 (high) FIXED - `unconfirm` checked only the requested id against `RemoteMappings` while removing the whole split group, so an unmapped fragment could drag out a MAPPED sibling and orphan its mapping, which then suppressed submit's duplicate guard and could allow a duplicate remote entry; the removal set is now computed first and the whole set is checked. F3 (low) FIXED - the guided path staged one removal per fragment (so removing two fragments of one group failed the apply) and miscounted the reviewed-empty flag; it now stages at most one removal per `SplitGroupID` and counts the intervals that would actually remain. F4 (low) FIXED - the offending id was recovered by string-parsing the wrapped error; typed errors now carry it and callers use `errors.As`. F5 (low) CARRIED AS AN OWNER QUESTION - `reset` blocks on `in_flight` only, while `internal/submit.isUnresolved` also treats `uncertain` and `unidentified` as unresolved; the owner specified `in_flight`, and the broader predicate lives in `internal/submit`, outside this slice's set, so widening it would either duplicate the predicate (the pattern the owner rejected in the previous slice) or require another boundary expansion. Bounded in practice: reset RETAINS the outcome and any mapping, and the submit restart gate still blocks replay, so no silent replacement occurs. F6 (low) FIXED - the skill did not document the `unconfirm_unknown_interval:<id>` refusal; it now does. F7 (low) CARRIED AS DOCUMENTED FOLLOW-UP - the guided path can still stage a set its own apply refuses when one split group has BOTH a staged removal and a staged edit on a different fragment (offer "Remove" for A, then "Edit" for B, or the reverse); the apply fails closed with `review: confirmed block not found:<id>` and writes NOTHING, so no half-applied state occurs, but the failure is a plain error rather than one of the stable reasons. Precise fix for the follow-up: for a fragment whose group is already staged for removal offer only Keep / "Removed with the rest", and drop or refuse edits targeting a staged-removal id before apply. F8 (low) CARRIED AS DOCUMENTED FOLLOW-UP - the guided prompt labels are not documented in the skill files (consistent with the pre-existing pattern of deferring guided prompts to the provenance-only `guided-workflow.md`), and `workflow.md` still says the mapped refusal fires "if the interval carries a remote mapping" when it now also fires when a split-group SIBLING is mapped and the id named is the mapped member's.
Independent review, re-review round 2: focused re-review of the F1-F4 fixes, read-only, lane @oracle `ses_ec9822f03ffej4ot4a6qIlfBmq` (the same lane reused within this session), on `ca9e525`. Verdict ACCEPTED. It independently reproduced each fix with `go -overlay` probes: the `scheduleAffecting` unit; that `mark_empty`+`unconfirm` and `mark_empty`+`reset` invalidate while `mark_empty` alone does not; that a refused apply persists nothing including the invalidation-then-refuse path; that the split-sibling removal is refused naming the mapped member with both intervals and the mapping intact; that a fully unmapped group is still removed whole; the `remainingConfirmedAfterRemovals` helper over ten cases; and the typed errors unwrapping with unchanged reason shapes. It re-answered all three safety questions negatively (no stale approval on any path, no lost history, no unguarded duplicate or silent replacement) and judged the F5 carry acceptable and not ship-blocking, because `submit.Execute`'s restart gate and the `create_duplicate_remote` plan guard bound it and reset retains the outcome and any mapping.
Verification: parent-run on the frozen two-commit snapshot `ca9e525`, log `.../T/opencode/review-undo-1-final-checks.log`: `gofmt -l` empty on the changed Go files; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go vet ./...` exit 0; full uncached `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0 across every package; `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...` exit 0; `-race` on `internal/run`, `internal/review`, `internal/workflow`, `internal/submit` and `cmd/timesheet` exit 0; the hermetic guided offline harness PASS; the new tests PASS; `git diff --check` clean. Every gate is pass, none is fail or not run, and none was retried. The first battery, on the pre-fix snapshot `11f7b03`, log `.../T/opencode/review-undo-1-checks.log`, was also fully green.
Boundaries reported, not crossed: Option B (a `workflow`-layer removal helper) was rejected because it would create a second removal path beside the existing unexported `removeConfirmed`; the F5 widening is not done here for the reason above.
Issue closure: #46 and #47 close only when this work is MERGED and the merged behaviour matches the approved design. The evidence for #46 is that a confirmed interval can now be removed by an `unconfirm` decision reachable from both the scripted and guided surfaces, with the submitted case deliberately BLOCKED and documented; the evidence for #47 is the `reset` decision key with its retention and refusal rules. Both were verified on the frozen snapshot by the tests above and by the review lanes; neither is closed by the commit alone.
Production readiness: unchanged. Production timesheet writes remain blocked, and live MCP, agent and Keychain write verification is still outstanding.
Next gate and blockers: the owner decides whether to merge this pull request. Production submission remains blocked.
```

```text
2026-10-14 / REVIEW-UNDO-1-F5 / authorized -> decided -> implemented -> verified -> re-reviewed -> records
Owner authority reference: owner-authorised MERGE-12/F5 packet (merge #51; then implement the broader unresolved predicate on a fresh branch from the merged `main`, with regression tests, the full battery on the frozen snapshot, and a focused re-review of the changed behaviour ONLY, because F5 is an incomplete guard on a brand-new feature rather than a regression; records; one pull request). Not authorised: merging the new pull request, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch, touching issues other than #46/#47 and new ones, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification, and fixing the gate non-hermeticity recorded below.
Decision, with the owner's rationale: reset blocks on ANY unresolved outcome, not only `in_flight`. An `uncertain` or `unidentified` outcome is a write whose success was never established, so allowing a reset to clear the day's review state while one exists would invite the operator to treat the day as clean when it is not, which the specification forbids when it requires an uncertain write to stop and be marked unresolved. `in_flight` alone is the narrower case of the same condition.
Implementation, one definition and one home: `internal/submit`'s predicate is EXPORTED as `IsUnresolved` with its body unchanged (a resolved row never needs reconciliation; `in_flight`, `uncertain` and `unidentified` do), and its doc now states it is the SINGLE definition, also used by the restart gate and the resolver. `review.ResetReview` drops its own narrow check (`workflow.UnresolvedOperations`, `in_flight` only) and the now-unused `ErrResetUnresolved` sentinel, because `internal/review` cannot import `internal/submit` (`internal/submit` imports `internal/review`), so no second copy of the rule can live in that layer. `internal/run` enforces the guard in the CALLER, before `ResetReview`, through `hasUnresolvedOutcome`, which loops `rec.Outcomes` and defers to `submit.IsUnresolved`. The refusal keeps the stable reason `reset_unresolved_submission` and now names all three statuses and the remedy. `workflow.UnresolvedOperations` is NOT widened, so the restart gate and the resolver keep their existing semantics. Exporting the predicate was practical exactly as the owner expected; the guard moved to the caller only because of the import direction, and no second copy of the rule exists.
Branch / HEAD / exact snapshot: `fix/review-undo-1-f5` from `main` `fb9c30b`; two commits: `acfd012` (the F5 fix, four files, +112/-21: `internal/submit/resolve.go`, `internal/review/review.go`, `internal/run/review.go`, `internal/run/review_test.go`) and `500dd20` (the re-review's two Low residuals, F5-D1/D2: `internal/run/submit.go` and `.claude/skills/timesheet/workflow.md`). No file outside the allowed set.
Regression tests, each demonstrated to fail before and pass after: an `in_flight`, an `uncertain` and an `unidentified` outcome each block the reset with the specific reason and write NOTHING (the confirmed interval and the outcome both survive); a RESOLVED uncertain outcome does NOT block, and the reset applies. Fail-before was demonstrated by temporarily restoring the narrow `in_flight`-only guard, which failed the uncertain and unidentified tests, then restoring the broad predicate byte-exactly.
Verification (parent-run on the frozen two-commit snapshot `500dd20`, log `.../T/opencode/review-undo-1-f5-final-checks.log`): `gofmt -l` empty on the changed Go files; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go vet ./...` exit 0; full uncached `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0 across every package including `internal/keychain`, so the #41 intermittent did not occur; `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...` exit 0; `-race` on `internal/run`, `internal/review`, `internal/submit` and `internal/workflow` exit 0; the hermetic guided offline harness PASS; the seven reset tests PASS; `git diff --check` clean. Every gate is pass, none is fail or not run, and none was retried. An earlier battery on the pre-residual snapshot `acfd012`, log `.../T/opencode/review-undo-1-f5-checks.log`, was also fully green.
Focused re-review: lane @oracle `ses_ec9822f03ffej4ot4a6qIlfBmq` (the lane that raised F5, reused within this session), read-only, on `acfd012`. Verdict ACCEPTED. It verified the exported predicate's body is byte-identical, that its two internal callers behave unchanged, that `workflow.UnresolvedOperations` was not widened so the restart gate and plan path keep their semantics, that the guard sits in the caller immediately before `ResetReview` and runs before any mutation so a refused reset writes nothing, and that `ResetReview` has exactly one production caller (`internal/run`'s `applyDecisions`), so no path can reset a day while an unresolved outcome exists. It confirmed the broadened guard with four overlay probes (in_flight/uncertain/unidentified all block with the stable reason and leave `Confirmed`, `Outcomes`, `Reviewed` and exclusions intact; a resolved row of any status does not block; the restart gate is unchanged). It judged the guard's relocation to the caller SOUND: `internal/submit` imports `internal/review`, so `internal/review` cannot call the exported predicate without a cycle, and the cost is that `ResetReview` now carries a documented precondition rather than being self-guarding, which is acceptable with one adjacent caller. Two Low residuals were raised and both were FIXED in `500dd20`: F5-D1, the skill still said an outcome unresolved "(in flight)" where the code now blocks on all three statuses; and F5-D2, `internal/run`'s `unresolvedKeySet` inlined a second copy of the three-status rule, which now delegates to `submit.IsUnresolved` so the "single definition, one home" claim is literally true rather than nearly true. The re-review's own note that this makes the single-definition claim exact is the reason D2 was fixed rather than carried.
Production readiness: unchanged. Production timesheet writes remain blocked, and live MCP, agent and Keychain write verification is still outstanding.
Next gate and blockers: the owner decides whether to merge this pull request. Production submission remains blocked.

2026-10-14 / LANE-ENGAGEMENT-1 / observer and librarian dispatched (one unavailable), recorded as evidence
The owner asked for two lanes that had never been used in this project. One ran; one does not exist in this runtime. Both outcomes are recorded because "not run" is not a pass and the substitution must be visible.

**`observer` - DISPATCHED AND REFUSED.** `subagent(agent:"observer")` returned `Unknown agent: observer`. `docs/orchestration/README.md:46` anticipated exactly this ("Present in the preset; dispatch only if the runtime exposes it"). **`librarian` - DISPATCHED, RAN, RETURNED.** Lane `ses_ec8393968ffeHgvXSY4NUTA4Wq`, read-only, verified the vendor bundle at `references/gradion-workspace-skill-antigravity/` against our repository's claims. Headline: the bundle is a skill for the Gradion Workspace MESSAGING product and contains ZERO timesheet tool schemas, deferring them to runtime discovery, so it cannot be the timesheet contract; our live-verified `tools/list` evidence stays authoritative. No vendor text contradicts any claim of ours, and nothing was inferred from silence. ```text
OBSERVER-SUBSTITUTE-1 / where a logged day's tokens actually go (measurement by the orchestrator because the observer lane is unavailable)
Method: the harness transcripts at `~/.pi/agent/sessions/--Users-huy.leminh-Projects-assignment-1--/*.jsonl`; each `message` carries `message.usage {input, output, cacheRead, cacheWrite, reasoning, totalTokens, cost}` and a model. A message is attributed to a phase by the `timesheet <subcommand>` token in its `bash` tool call; everything else is "agent turns (other)". Consumed excludes reasoning, per the package contract. Cache hit = cacheRead/(input+cacheRead).
Results (consumed tokens, USD from the transcript's own cost field):
- 2026-10-08 run (session `2026-10-11T15-41`, 128 records): preflight 1 turn 18,683; review 8 turns 436,893; classify 9 turns 431,795; preview 1 turn 53,653; submit 2 turns 143,203; agent turns 39 turns 1,471,218. TOTAL 2,555,445 tok, $0.1011, cacheHit 97.0%.
- 2026-10-06 run (session `2026-10-12T11-01`, 235 records, spanning 2026-10-12T11:01Z to 2026-10-13T07:57Z): preflight 14,252; review 183,834; classify 232,475; preview 159,892; approval 211,653; submit 40,276; resolve 218,159; export 60,159; agent turns 82 turns 3,636,411. TOTAL 4,757,111 tok, $0.1815, cacheHit 96.5%.
- 2026-10-07 (session `2026-10-13T11-16`, 115 records): preview 1 turn 84,857; agent turns 47 turns 2,374,951. TOTAL 2,459,808 tok, $0.0538, cacheHit 92.6%. This session REVIEWED 2026-10-07 and ran ONE preview; it did not submit.
NOT MEASURED, and why: the 2026-10-07 first live write has NO transcript in either format the adapters parse. No pi session covers 2026-10-13 15:00-15:30Z, which is when the day's record and scratch files were written, and `~/.claude` holds no transcripts. That run was driven outside the pi harness, so its tokens are unrecoverable from the stores `internal/metrics` can read. Also NOT attributable: `collection` and `proposal`, because there is no `timesheet collect` or `timesheet propose` subcommand, so one `review`/`run` invocation performs both and a transcript can only attribute their combined cost; this is the gap the package's per-STEP accounting was meant to close, and the per-step store is EMPTY (no run has ever been imported). `cacheWrite` is 0 in every session measured.
Findings that shaped METRICS-2: `model` and `cost` are already present per message and `~/.pi/agent/models-store.json` is a usable price catalog, so price needs no new dependency; cache hit is derivable (92.6-97.0% here) and is not currently reported; sessions genuinely span days (the 2026-10-06 session crossed local midnight), which is the owner's question 1 and 2 backed by a measured example; reasoning is a real but small share and is already excluded.

```text
2026-10-14 / METRICS-2 / authorized -> designed -> boundary-reported -> implemented -> verified -> reviewed -> records
Owner authority reference: owner-authorised MERGE-13/METRICS-2 packet (merge #53; a design pass and implementation for issue #55; dispatch `observer` and `librarian`; records; one pull request). Not authorised: merging the new pull request, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch, touching issues other than #55 and new ones filed, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification.
Requirement (issue #55): count every token from the start of the session used to log work for a day, until the work is logged on Gradion, and store per session Input, Output, Reasoning, Cache Read, Cache Write, Cache Hit (%), Total Tokens, plus model and price.
Design verdict (lane @oracle `ses_ec839396bffersZD6zxYhmYqqq`, read-only): CHANGES REQUIRED, THEN ACCEPTED. The `internal/metrics` foundation is sound and the dependency and honesty constraints hold, but the requirement as literally written is NOT faithfully representable against the real data. Three verified facts forced the design: a real session used TWO models, so "one model per session" is false in practice; a model in real use (`space-bunny-free` under `opencode-go`) is ABSENT from the price catalog, so its price must be unknown rather than free; and a real session spans local midnight (20.9 h), which is exactly the owner's cited case. The owner's design questions were answered as follows.
  1. What defines a session's scope: THE HARNESS SESSION, one transcript identified by its session id. Rejected: the workflow record's lifetime (it is day-keyed by construction and carries no session id anywhere, so it cannot be a session, and one record is touched by many sessions), and first-collection-to-last-outcome (nothing in the data links a collection or an outcome to a session, so that interval would FABRICATE a link, which the package's fail-closed rule forbids).
  2. How a session is associated with exactly one day: an association key of session -> logged day, with ambiguity represented as UNKNOWN and never guessed. Many-to-one (a day logged across several sessions) is allowed and is summed; one-to-many (a session touching two days) has no single day and is unknown.
  3. How an unknown price is represented honestly: the package's EXISTING nil-vs-pointer-to-zero idiom — all four rates are `*float64`, nil is unknown and a non-nil pointer to 0 is a known zero, and the derived cost is `*float64`, nil when any contributing count or rate is unknown. This mirrors `CallCost` and `MetricComparison.Percent` and invents no second unknown-vs-zero type, which ADR-0006 forbids.
BOUNDARY REPORTED, and the one thing this session would not decide alone: the design's PREFERRED association was to read the `--date` value out of a transcript TOOL CALL. ADR-0006's Safety rule states that an adapter extracts "only counters, ids, timestamps, the `timesheet` subcommand token, and the provider-native NUMERIC cost" and that "Message text, tool arguments, and tool results are never decoded into a persisted field." Capturing `--date` from a tool call IS decoding a tool argument into a persisted field, so the design's preferred association is FORBIDDEN by an existing safety rule, and amending a safety ADR is not something this session may do unilaterally. The implementation therefore uses the ADR-COMPLIANT substitute: the day is supplied explicitly by the operator as `metrics import --date D` (Association `import_flag`), and failing that is derived from the distinct LOCAL DATES OF THE SESSION'S WORKFLOW-STEP CALLS, which are timestamps and the already-derived StepID — both expressly permitted by the ADR. Exactly one local date -> that day (`local_step_date`); zero, or two or more -> unknown. CONSEQUENCE, stated plainly for the owner: the cited midnight-straddling case resolves to UNKNOWN, and resolving it automatically would require the owner to authorise an ADR-0006 amendment permitting a validated date value to be persisted. NO ADR WAS AMENDED, and the safety rule stands true as written.
Behaviour: a `SessionRecord` is built from an already-parsed `RunRecord`, resolves a per-`(provider, model)` breakdown against the runtime catalog, derives the association without decoding any tool argument, and is stored idempotently under `<metrics-dir>/sessions/`. Session metrics are aggregated into a SEPARATE lane on `DailyAggregate` (`SessionCount`, `SessionIDs`, `SessionUsage`, `SessionTotalTokens`, `SessionCacheHitPct`, `SessionCost`, `UnassociatedSessions`), never merged with `Orchestration`/`Editorial`. The CLI gains `metrics sessions [--date|--session] [--format]`, and `metrics import` gains `--model-catalog` (default `$PI_MODEL_CATALOG`, else `~/.pi/agent/models-store.json`) and `--timezone`; a missing or unparseable catalog WARNS and continues with an unknown price at exit 0. Cache hit is `cacheRead / (input + cacheRead + cacheWrite)`, nil when the denominator is zero or a component is unknown.
Sessions whose day is unknown are NOT folded into any day's totals: folding an ambiguous session into every day its calls touch would double-count a midnight-straddling session across BOTH days. They stay VISIBLE as `UnassociatedSessions` and in `metrics sessions`. This rule was corrected after the first implementation pass, which had folded them in; the correction and its fail-before are recorded below.
Files changed: `internal/metrics/{models,catalog,session,store,aggregate,report,compare}.go` and `internal/metrics/session_test.go` (catalog.go, session.go, session_test.go new); `cmd/timesheet/metrics.go` and `cmd/timesheet/metrics_test.go`; `.claude/skills/timesheet/{SKILL.md,workflow.md}`. NO ADR was changed and no file outside those was touched.
Tests: 13 new tests. The core's 8 cover the cache-hit bands and unknown component, unknown-price-is-nil-not-zero with a JSON `null` and a known-zero `cacheWrite`, the midnight-straddling association (unknown) versus a single local date, the multi-model breakdown, the `(provider, model)` catalog key, session/orchestration separation with reasoning excluded, session write idempotence, and the unknown-price table rendering. `TestAggregate_UnassociatedSessionNotFoldedIntoDay` guards the folding rule and failed before the correction (`sessionsForDate(2026-10-13) = [s-m-assoc s-straddle], want only [s-m-assoc]`). The CLI's 5 cover an import writing a session with a resolved price, an absent model producing `"cost":null` and `null` rates, a missing catalog warning and still succeeding, the table rendering `unknown` rather than `0`, and the session JSON. Fail-before for the CLI set was captured by stashing only `cmd/timesheet/metrics.go`.
Verification (parent-run on the frozen two-commit snapshot `beb19c9`, log `.../T/opencode/metrics-2-final-checks.log`): `gofmt -l` empty on the changed Go files; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go vet ./...` exit 0; full uncached `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0 across every package including `internal/keychain`, so the #41 intermittent did not occur; `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...` exit 0; `-race` on `internal/metrics`, `cmd/timesheet` and `internal/workflow` exit 0; the hermetic guided offline harness PASS; the 12 metrics and 5 CLI METRICS-2 tests PASS; `git diff --check` clean. Every gate is pass, none is fail or not run, and none was retried. An earlier battery on the pre-fix snapshot `7a9b475`, log `.../T/opencode/metrics-2-checks.log`, was also fully green.
Independent review: lane @oracle `ses_ec80971e7ffeCiLjoeouwZiSo0`, read-only, on `7a9b475`. Verdict ACCEPTED, no correctness defect against the acceptance contract. It independently verified the ADR-0006 safety claim adversarially (only `arguments.command` is decoded, solely for the subcommand token; `BuildSession`/`associate` use only `c.Timestamp` and the already-derived `c.Step`, and no `--date` value is ever read), that no new render site can print `0` where the value is unknown, that `applySessions` still works when called directly and cannot be stale on the real path, that the cache-hit definition matches, that a multi-model session leaves `Model` empty with a breakdown, that the catalog key is two-level `(provider, model)`, that `omitempty` does not hide a known zero cost, that the 14 tests claim only what they verify, and that the dependency, editorial/orchestration separation, reasoning-exclusion, context-length and SchemaVersion constraints all hold. Seven low findings, dispositioned: F1 FIXED (a catalog entry present with no cost block was labelled `price_source=catalog` while all four rates were unknown; an all-nil resolved price is now unknown); F2 FIXED (a real usability bug: `WriteSession` keyed idempotency on the content hash only, so re-importing the same transcript with a corrected `--date` was a silent no-op and the operator's explicit day was never applied; the decision now also considers `LoggedDate`/`Association`, while a byte-identical repeat stays a no-op); F3 FIXED (the skill now states the unassociated count is per-day and touch-scoped, and that an unassociated session with no dated workflow-step calls is visible only through `metrics sessions`); F4 FIXED (documented `applySessions`'s precondition); F5 FIXED (corrected the `session_cost` omitempty comment, which was true only while the cost is nil). F6 CARRIED (the pre-existing `workflow.Measurement` JSON idiom `{known,value}` requires a consumer to read `known`; package-wide, not introduced here). F7 CARRIED AS A FOLLOW-UP (no ADR records the METRICS-2 session contract; `docs/adr/0006` is METRICS-1-era and silent on session records and pricing; writing one was not authorised and no ADR was touched).
Production readiness: unchanged. Production timesheet writes remain blocked, and live MCP, agent and Keychain write verification is still outstanding.
Focused re-review of the fixes: lane @oracle `ses_ec80971e7ffeCiLjoeouwZiSo0` (the lane that raised the findings, reused within this session), read-only, on `beb19c9`. Verdict ACCEPTED: F1-F5 all resolved with genuine fail-before tests, no new defect, and specifically no write-loop or unbounded-rewrite risk from the F2 idempotency change (import is deterministic and `Store.Aggregate` never writes sessions, so a corrected day costs exactly one write and later identical re-imports no-op).
Next gate and blockers: the owner decides whether to merge this pull request. Production submission remains blocked.
```

```text
2026-10-14 / DECISIONS-2 / two owner decisions recorded as accepted limitations
Both were given in the TOKEN-EFFICIENCY-1 packet and are recorded here so nobody re-litigates them.
1. `observer` IS NOT EXPOSED BY THIS RUNTIME. The lane is declared in the oh-my-opencode-slim preset and in `docs/orchestration/README.md`, but `subagent(agent:"observer")` returns `Unknown agent: observer`. The standing instruction to use every lane is therefore satisfied UP TO WHAT THE RUNTIME PROVIDES, and nobody should chase the lane again. The measurement it was to perform was done by the orchestrator, read-only, and stays labelled a SUBSTITUTION in every record. Recorded as a runtime limitation, not a project defect.
2. ADR-0006 STANDS AND IS NOT AMENDED. The METRICS-2 design wanted to read a `--date` value out of transcript tool calls to associate a session with a day. ADR-0006's Safety rule bars decoding tool arguments into a persisted field, and the owner keeps that rule intact: a narrow whitelist would probably be safe, but the rule earns its keep by being absolute. ACCEPTED LIMITATION, with the lever noted: the derived association is the local date of the session's own workflow-step calls, so a session that straddles local midnight resolves to `unknown`, and a session that logs a PAST day associates with the date it RAN rather than the day it logged. The remedy, already documented in the skill, is the operator-stated `metrics import --date D`. The owner may authorise an ADR-0006 amendment later if they want the association automated.

```text
2026-10-14 / TOKEN-EFFICIENCY-1 / authorized -> measured -> instrument-fixed -> re-measured -> reviewed -> records
Owner authority reference: owner-authorised TOKEN-EFFICIENCY-1 packet (measure the turns before changing anything; propose the smallest changes that remove turns with their safety or clarity cost; implement only what the measurement justifies and re-measure with the same method; populate the metrics store with an authorised read-only import; records; one pull request). Not authorised: merging the pull request, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch, touching issues other than #54 and new ones filed, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification, amending any ADR, or emitting transcript content.
THE TURN-LEVEL MEASUREMENT, taken before any change, by the same method as OBSERVER-SUBSTITUTE-1 (the harness transcripts at `~/.pi/agent/sessions/--Users-huy.leminh-Projects-assignment-1--`, attributing each assistant turn by the `timesheet <subcommand>` token in its `bash` call, everything else to agent turns):
  - 2026-10-08 run: 60 turns, 2,555,445 tokens, 42,590 tokens/turn, $0.1011.
  - 2026-10-06 run: 114 turns, 4,757,111 tokens, 41,729 tokens/turn, $0.1815.
  - 2026-10-07 review: 48 turns, 2,459,808 tokens, 51,246 tokens/turn, $0.0538.
  The single most important number: tokens per turn is roughly CONSTANT at 42-51k and is dominated by `cacheRead` (the top single turns are 70-95k, of which 68-90k is a re-read of an unchanged prefix). Prompt length is therefore NOT the lever; the NUMBER OF TURNS is.
RANKED CONTRIBUTORS, with numbers (share of that session's tokens):
  1. DISCOVERY by the agent (grep/cat/ls/find): 32.1% (25 turns), 40.4% (44 turns), 43.9% (23 turns).
  2. The agent reading files (`read`): 2.7% (2 turns), 5.8% (7 turns), 36.7% (18 turns).
  3. The timesheet CLI's own steps: 42.4% (21 turns), 23.6% (32 turns), 3.4% (1 turn).
  4. Pure reasoning/text with no tool call: 11.9%, 12.5%, 9.7%.
  5. Writing/editing files: 0%, 10.8% (11 turns), 4.4%.
  6. git: 3.8%, 4.8%, 10.4%.
  The single biggest in-run lever was therefore NOT the pipeline: in the largest run, 31 of 44 discovery turns were the agent reading REPOSITORY SOURCE (`internal/run/`, `internal/...`), which is exactly the defect SKILL-BOUNDARY-1 fixed on 2026-10-13. ALL THREE measured sessions PREDATE that fix, so the baseline is stale by construction and the top lever has already been pulled; a true post-fix baseline needs a logging run this slice cannot perform (it would require live Gradion access and a production write, neither authorised). Stated as a limit, not glossed.
THE CHANGE, and why it is the one the measurement justifies: the instrument itself was broken, which is why the per-step view was unusable and why #55's association could not resolve. `internal/metrics/attribute.go` recognised a `timesheet` subcommand ONLY as a direct first-token invocation, so of 44 real commands mentioning `timesheet`/`ts` in one session, ZERO were recognised; EVERY `CallRecord.Step` was `StepUnknown`. The rule now scans every token for one whose basename is `timesheet` or `ts`, in an invocation position (first token, after `go run`, or after a shell separator), immediately followed by a recognised subcommand token. A path that merely contains `timesheet` (`cat .../timesheet/workflow.md`, `ls cmd/timesheet`) and a bare `timesheet`/`ts` with no subcommand still resolve to `StepUnknown`, and the pre-existing grep/echo false-positive guards still hold. Only the subcommand token is returned, so ADR-0006's Safety rule is unchanged. A skill clarification was added too: the derived association is the session's RUN date, so a past-day log must pass `--date`.
RE-MEASURED DELTA, same method, on one real transcript with throwaway metrics dirs: calls with a non-`unknown` step 0 -> 8 of 60; session `local_dates` `[]` -> `["2026-10-11"]`; `logged_date` `""` -> `2026-10-11`; `association` `unknown` -> `local_step_date`; run `state` `unknown` -> `completed`. This is the provable delta for THIS slice: the instrument now populates and the session association resolves. The TURN-COUNT delta of the efficiency work itself is NOT measurable here, because it needs a post-fix logging run; that limitation is recorded rather than papered over with a derived number.
No safety gate was removed, no confirmation gate was touched, and no prompt was shortened in a way that leaves the interface incomplete. No transcript content was emitted anywhere: only counts, step ids, dates and token/price aggregates.
METRICS IMPORT (owner-authorised by issue #34, read-only, aggregate only): the store `~/.timesheet/metrics` was EMPTY and has now been populated from three real transcripts, with NO transcript content printed or committed. After the instrument fix, all three sessions resolve: `2026-10-11` (2,555,445 tok, 97.0% cache hit, $0.1011, model `space-bunny`), `2026-10-12` (4,757,111 tok, 96.5%, price unknown because one of its two models is absent from the catalog), `2026-10-13` (2,459,808 tok, 92.6%, $0.0538). The re-import also proved the METRICS-2 F2 re-association fix in production: the stored sessions were rewritten from `unknown` to a resolved day.
Files changed: `internal/metrics/attribute.go`, `internal/metrics/attribute_test.go`, `.claude/skills/timesheet/workflow.md`, `docs/orchestration/state.md`. No ADR was amended and nothing outside those was touched.
Tests: three new attribution tests (`TestAttributeCommand_CompoundInvocation`, `TestAttributeCommand_PathContainingTimesheetIsNotAStep`, `TestAttributeCommand_NoFalsePositiveWithoutSubcommand`). One existing table case (`compound_cd_prefix`) necessarily changed expectation from `StepUnknown` to `StepCollect`, because it is the exact shape the fix targets; every other existing assertion is unchanged and passing.
Verification (parent-run on the frozen snapshot `daabb98`, log `.../T/opencode/token-efficiency-1-checks.log`): `gofmt -l` empty on the changed Go files; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go vet ./...` exit 0; full uncached `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0 across every package including `internal/keychain`, so the #41 intermittent did not occur; `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...` exit 0; `-race` on `internal/metrics` and `cmd/timesheet` exit 0; the hermetic guided offline harness PASS; every attribution test PASS (15 table cases, 4 compound shapes, 2 path cases, 3 no-false-positive cases); `git diff --check` clean. Every gate is pass, none is fail or not run, and none was retried.
Independent review: lane @oracle `ses_ec7e8a684ffe9kFyIMPzA5iLGE`, read-only, on `daabb98`. Verdict CHANGES REQUIRED on one point only; the code change itself was accepted as correct, pure, safety-compliant and well-covered, and the changed existing table case was confirmed correct rather than weakened. It adversarially confirmed the false-positive guards (`cat .../timesheet/workflow.md`, `ls cmd/timesheet`, `grep -r timesheet submit`, `echo timesheet review`, `"$T/ts" --help`, `$TS`/`${TS}`), the ADR-0006 Safety property (only the subcommand token is returned, nothing else retained or logged), purity, the baseline-predates-SKILL-BOUNDARY-1 claim as checkable and the conclusion as honestly stated, and the new skill sentence against `session.go`'s association logic.
Review findings and dispositions: F1 (medium) RECORDED, NOT AMENDED, which is the reviewer's own alternative. `docs/adr/0006-metrics-contract.md` states that attribution happens "ONLY when the command's FIRST token is the `timesheet`/`ts` binary (or the `go run ./cmd/timesheet` form)", and this slice deliberately widens that heuristic. The owner's Decision 2 is that ADR-0006 STANDS AND IS NOT AMENDED, so the ADR was NOT touched and the discrepancy is recorded here explicitly instead: ADR-0006's ATTRIBUTION-HEURISTIC clause is now STALE and describes a rule the code no longer implements, while its SAFETY clause remains true and intact. Amending the heuristic clause needs owner authorisation; this is the flag for it. F2 (low) CARRIED AS A DOCUMENTED HEURISTIC LIMIT: the position guard does not parse quoting or heredocs, so a separator inside a quoted string or a heredoc body can promote a mention to invocation position in narrow contrived shapes (`echo "x && ts review "`); `echo "x && ts review"` is saved by the glued closing quote. F3 (low) CARRIED: a separator glued to the binary token (`"$T/ts"; review`) is a false NEGATIVE, which is the safe direction. F4 (info) CARRIED: the binary token is matched case-insensitively but the subcommand token is not, so `TS Review` resolves the binary and not the subcommand. F5 (low, PRE-EXISTING, not introduced) noted: `hasFlag(args, "--decisions")` reads a tool argument, which is pre-existing from the base and persists nothing beyond the derived StepID. The reviewer also recorded a verification limit worth carrying: the numeric measurement (per-turn cost, the ranked percentages) is not committed as a repository artifact, so its percentages are taken on trust while the predate assertion is independently checkable.
Production readiness: unchanged. Production timesheet writes remain blocked, and live MCP, agent and Keychain write verification is still outstanding.
Next gate and blockers: the owner decides whether to merge this pull request. Production submission remains blocked.

```text
2026-10-14 / ADR-0006-AMEND-1 / authorized -> amended -> confirmed
Owner authority reference: owner-authorised ADR amendment (amend ADR-0006's ATTRIBUTION clause, and only that clause, with a dated in-place supersession; confirm the Safety clause is untouched and that no other statement became false; get a focused read-only confirmation from the lane that raised the finding; commit on a branch from the merged `main`). Not authorised: amending any other clause, adding a new ADR, touching the Safety rule, or implementing anything else.
What was amended, and why: the attribution paragraph required a call to be attributed ONLY when the command's FIRST token was the `timesheet`/`ts` binary (or the `go run ./cmd/timesheet` form). Measured on the owner's real transcripts, ZERO of 44 commands mentioning `timesheet`/`ts` in one session were recognised under it, because real invocations are compound shell commands, so every `CallRecord.Step` was `StepUnknown` and the per-step lane carried no information. A stale sentence in an ADR is the drift this project keeps paying for, so it is superseded rather than left.
Method: the original paragraph is RETAINED as history and ONE dated bullet (`**The attribution clause above is SUPERSEDED, 2026-10-14.**`) was added in place. It states the rule now in effect (a token whose basename is `timesheet` or `ts`, in an INVOCATION POSITION, first token / after `go run` / immediately after a shell separator, IMMEDIATELY followed by a recognised subcommand token), the measured reason, and every guard that still holds (`grep -r timesheet submit` and `echo timesheet review` still not attributed; a path merely containing `timesheet` still not attributed; `ls cmd/timesheet` excluded by position; a bare `timesheet`/`ts` with no following subcommand still `StepUnknown`; category bucketing unchanged). It states EXPLICITLY that the Safety rule is NOT affected and WHY the two clauses are separate: Safety governs WHAT MAY BE PERSISTED (only counters, ids, timestamps, the subcommand TOKEN, and the provider-native numeric cost; never message text, tool arguments or tool results), while attribution governs HOW A STEP IS DERIVED from a command already being inspected, and the widened rule still RETURNS ONLY the subcommand token so it persists no more than before. It also records that the change refused on Safety grounds is a DIFFERENT one that remains refused: decoding a `--date` VALUE out of a tool argument to associate a session with a day.
The Safety clause is BYTE-UNCHANGED. The amendment is 28 insertions and ZERO deletions, so no existing sentence was edited or removed.
Focused confirmation: the lane that raised the finding, @oracle `ses_ec7e8a684ffe9kFyIMPzA5iLGE`, read-only, CONFIRMED all four points: the Safety clause is byte-identical; the amendment is a dated supersession in place with no new ADR and no other clause edited; the new text matches the implemented rule in `internal/metrics/attribute.go` and still excludes all five named shapes; and NO other statement in ADR-0006 became false, including the remaining attribution sentences and the unrelated "Additive model fields" bullet. It flagged two wording imprecisions, both TIGHTENED before the commit: the binary basename is matched case-insensitively while the subcommand token is matched case-sensitively (now stated, and it is the safe direction), and `ls cmd/timesheet` is excluded by POSITION rather than by "merely contains" (now stated correctly).
Branch / commit: `docs/adr-0006-attribution-amendment`, commit `b1046bb`, `docs/adr/0006-metrics-contract.md` only. No other file changed. No ADR was added and the Safety clause was not touched.
Known gap, NOT closed by this amendment and recorded so it is not mistaken for coverage: ADR-0006 is still silent on the METRICS-2 session contract (session records, the association rule, the price/catalog honesty rule). That remains carried debt from the METRICS-2 review's F7; this packet authorised only the attribution clause.

```text
2026-10-14 / RECORDS-1 / design pass ONLY, not implemented (owner-directed)
Owner authority reference: owner-authorised RECORDS-1 design pass for issue #56 (design pass and verdict only; report with its answers to the five questions, its risks and the honest cost, and STOP before any implementation; say plainly if the approach is wrong). Not authorised: any implementation, any vault write, any ADR change, any file change outside the records.
Design verdict (lane @oracle `ses_ec7cdaeb9ffedxC7z31L6CjZgX`, read-only): PROCEED WITH CHANGES. The direction is right, but not as the packet specifies it, and the packet's own motivating examples are partly OUT OF REACH of the proposed mechanism.
Verified current state: the duplicated narrative is large and concrete. `docs/orchestration/state.md` is 1,976 lines of append-only ledger; the vault's `Project - assignment-1.md` is 533 lines / 132,156 bytes with about twenty slice sections that restate it; the daily notes are 37-75 KB each. The SAME slice status is restated in FOUR places, verified for METRICS-2 (issue #55): the repo ledger `state.md:1896-1914`, the vault project note `:440-454`, the daily log `2026-10-14.md:27-37`, and the dashboard `00 - Dashboards/Performance & Impact Hub.md:39`.
The four drift instances, SORTED BY WHETHER GENERATION CAN FIX THEM: (1) the stale merged-range figure in the dashboard that contradicted its own next sentence, and (3) the four-way slice-status restatement, are REPO-VERSUS-VAULT drift and generation removes them. (2) the daily-note Summary superseded by appended corrections, and (4) the two competing session-range bullets for the same session in one daily note, are drift INSIDE AUTHORED EVIDENCE and generation does NOT fix them.
Answer 1, generated versus authored: GENERATED is the vault project note BODY (status line, milestone table, ADR and link index, current blockers) plus per-ADR compacts, a lessons-learned digest, a generated index, and the dashboard's assignment-1 row. AUTHORED, and the generator must NEVER write: every `02 - Daily & Logs/*.md` in full, because its Work-journal and Daily-impact bullets are TIMESHEET EVIDENCE whose identity is a content hash (editing one creates a new evidence item), and because the collector reads those headings; the existing vault ADR series `ADR-001..007`; `06 - Knowledge & Wiki/*`; `99 - Meta/*`; other projects. The line as a rule: the generator owns the PROJECT-LEVEL SUMMARY AND CROSS-LINKS, the human owns TIME-STAMPED EVIDENCE AND DECISIONS. Rejected: generating the daily Summary too, which is technically safe (that heading is not collected) but puts a generated block inside an authored evidence file and blurs the one boundary the design depends on; offered instead as an explicit owner option.
Answer 2, what it reads, writes and how it is invoked: it must read a NEW small hand-maintained manifest `docs/orchestration/status.yaml` (one entry per slice/merge/decision: id, kind, state, commits, pr, issues, date, one_line) plus the ADR frontmatter and a NEW curated `docs/lessons-learned.md`, and `git rev-parse HEAD`. It WRITES only the generated vault paths and never touches `state.md`, any ADR, or any daily note. It is Go at `scripts/vaultgen/main.go` with a `scripts/sync-vault.sh` wrapper (`--write` / `--check`), invoked as ONE command, adding NO new dependency because the Go toolchain is already the gate. THE MANIFEST IS THE LOAD-BEARING DECISION: parsing 1,976 lines of free prose with heuristics would be brittle and would silently produce wrong milestones, which MOVES the drift instead of removing it. Rejected: parsing the ledger prose; reading only git log plus ADR frontmatter (git knows merges but not "PR open / source-accepted / blocked", which is exactly the status that drifts).
Answer 3, the stamp: every generated file opens with a do-not-edit HTML header naming the source path, the source commit and the date, and a machine-readable frontmatter block (`generated`, `generator`, `source`, `source_commit`, `source_date`, `generated_at`, `body_sha256`). A HAND EDIT IS DETECTED BY HASH, not by hope: on regeneration the generator strips the header, hashes the remainder, compares it to the file's own `body_sha256`, and if it differs it REFUSES to overwrite, prints the file name, and exits non-zero.
Answer 4, drift detection GOING FORWARD (three layers, in order of reliability): (a) THE STAMP ITSELF, always on and free: every generated file names its source commit, so a file generated before the latest repo commit is VISIBLY behind to any reader without running anything; (b) the honest answer the packet asked for, A CHECK THAT FAILS WHEN A GENERATED FILE DISAGREES WITH ITS SOURCE: `./scripts/sync-vault.sh --check` regenerates in memory, compares each file to disk, and exits non-zero listing each differing file and whether the cause is staleness or a hand edit; (c) a generated index showing the whole set's freshness at once. Where it runs: the vault is OUTSIDE the repo, so NO CI CAN SEE IT, and the design says so rather than pretending otherwise; it runs on demand and as a required step in `commit-and-vault.md`'s vault-synchronization section, which already defines a `vault-pending` state. HONEST LIMITS, stated rather than oversold: if the human never runs the check only the visible stamp protects them, and the stamp does not block; and the check CANNOT catch a WRONG generator, because the same generator produces both sides, so golden-file unit tests are REQUIRED rather than optional.
Answer 5, the existing vault content: the project note BODY is REPLACED by the generated compact only AFTER its hand-written body is ARCHIVED to `01 - Projects/archive/Project - assignment-1 (authored history to 2026-10-14).md` with a stamped historical header, written once and never touched again; the dashboard's assignment-1 row is replaced; the DAILY NOTES ARE LEFT ALONE because they are evidence; the existing vault ADR series is LEFT ALONE and the generated per-ADR compacts live in a new subfolder; `06 - Knowledge & Wiki`, `99 - Meta` and other projects are LEFT ALONE. A reader is warned that a note is historical by TWO signals, the archive file's stamped header and the generated index's current-versus-historical mapping, and NEVER by editing an authored note to add a warning, because that would itself be a hand edit of authored content. SYMLINKS: the Obsidian caveat was CONFIRMED in the vault (no symlinks present today; Smart Connections indexes `.smart-env/`; an external symlink is not indexed), so NO SYMLINK CARRIES INDEXED CONTENT: the generated compacts are REAL FILES inside the vault, which is what the index and dataview can see. At most ONE symlink is justified, a human-navigation shortcut labelled as not indexed. Explicitly rejected: symlinking the repo's `state.md`/ADRs into the vault as the MECHANISM, because they would be invisible to the index and the purpose would be defeated.
Risks, failure modes and the honest cost: a WRONG generator is the top risk and the check cannot catch it, so golden tests and human reading are the only defences. A vault hand edit is loud (hash mismatch, refuse, exit non-zero). If the check is never run, only the stamp protects, and nothing blocks. A renamed source makes a stamp 404, so named sources must not be renamed. The MAIN RESIDUAL DRIFT is manifest-versus-ledger: `status.yaml` and `state.md` are BOTH hand-maintained and can disagree; it is at least inside the repo where checks and review live, but it is NOT zero. Honest cost: a careful Go generator plus golden tests plus `--check` plus the wrapper is roughly one to two focused days, plus authoring `status.yaml` and `lessons-learned.md` and a one-time archive; it BREAKS the project note's hand-written history out of its place, changes the dashboard row, and adds a mandatory check step and a second thing to keep current; the owner LOSES the ability to hand-write the project note, which is the intent but a real loss of a writing surface; the owner KEEPS the daily evidence, the ADRs, the dashboards, dataview and the index, which are the vault's genuinely unique value.
On rejecting the approach: the design considered the strongest objection, that the vault's value IS its authored narrative, and rejected rejection, because that history is DUPLICATED rather than unique (the repo already holds it) and because the vault's unique value is cross-project dashboards, dataview and the daily evidence log, none of which this design touches. BUT it states plainly that the packet MUST STOP CLAIMING the generator fixes the daily-note drift: instances (2) and (4) are inside authored evidence and are fixed only by authoring discipline, or by a separate mechanism.
First reversible step, if the owner wants to start small: add `status.yaml` and `lessons-learned.md`, add a generator that produces ONE NEW file beside the existing project note, and REPLACE NOTHING. That proves the stamp, the hand-edit detection, the check and determinism with zero destruction, and is fully reversible by deleting two files.
Open questions put to the owner, unactioned: (a) daily-note drift needs a separate mechanism (a generated Summary block, or a boundary linter) or is accepted as authoring discipline; (b) is a second hand-maintained manifest acceptable, or should the ledger's update-record template be reshaped into a parseable block instead; (c) whether to have the single navigation symlink at all; (d) whether the vault will be put under git or a backup schedule BEFORE the first replace, which is a BLOCKING prerequisite because the vault has no history today; (e) whether a new curated `docs/lessons-learned.md` is acceptable as the digest source; (f) whether the dashboard row becomes a dataview or a generated marked block.
NOTHING WAS IMPLEMENTED. No vault file was written, no generator was added, no ADR was changed by this design pass, and the design is recorded here so the owner can decide whether to proceed.
Next gate and blockers: the owner decides whether to proceed, and if so with which first step. Production submission remains blocked.

```text
2026-10-14 / DECISIONS-3 / three owner decisions recorded, including a correction of the packet by the design
1. THE DESIGN'S CORRECTION IS ACCEPTED, AND IT CORRECTS THE PACKET. The RECORDS-1 packet claimed generation would fix the drift class. The design pass showed it does NOT fix two of the four cited instances, because both live inside AUTHORED EVIDENCE: the superseded daily-note Summary, and the competing session-range bullets for one session in one note. The wrong claim is the owner's packet, and it is recorded here as the source of the error rather than as an error of the design or the session. TWO CONVENTIONS FOLLOW, owned by the owner and to be applied in the vault BY THE OWNER, not by this session: (a) a daily note's Summary becomes a POINTER rather than a narrative, because narrative there is what drifts and because the collected headings are the work-journal and daily-impact sections anyway; (b) ONE RANGE BULLET PER SESSION PER DAY, with later corrections appended as additive bullets rather than as a second competing range. Neither convention was applied in this packet; both are recorded as owner-owned.
2. VAULT HISTORY IS TAKEN BY ARCHIVE, NOT BY `git init`. The design is right that a replace must be preceded by recoverable history, and the owner will not initialise a git repository inside the owner's vault without asking. The convention for this project is therefore: take a FRESH TIMESTAMPED ARCHIVE of `~/ai-database` immediately before ANY vault write, into the same backup directory used at handover (`~/backups/assignment-1/YYYY-MM-DD/`). `git init` is recorded as an option the owner MAY choose later; it was not performed.
3. GOLDEN TESTS ARE MANDATORY FOR THE GENERATOR. The session's own finding is that the drift check CANNOT catch a wrong generator, because the same generator produces both sides. The generator therefore ships with committed golden fixtures, so a wrong generator FAILS A TEST rather than silently writing a plausible file. This was implemented in RECORDS-1 step one, not deferred.
Next gate and blockers: none. Production submission remains blocked.

```text
2026-10-14 / RECORDS-1-STEP-ONE / authorized -> implemented (step one only) -> verified -> records
Owner authority reference: owner-authorised RECORDS-1 step-one packet (implement EXACTLY the design's first reversible step; nothing replaces anything; the manifest, the lessons source, the generator with --write/--check and no new dependency, the stamp with body_sha256 and the hand-edit refusal, and ONE new vault file beside the existing project note; take the archive BEFORE any vault write; verify the four checks; then STOP and report). Not authorised: replacing the project note, touching the dashboard row, archiving anything else, answering the other open design questions by implementing them, merging the pull request, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch, touching any issue, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification.
What was implemented, all inside the repo except the one generated vault file:
- `docs/orchestration/status.yaml`: the small hand-maintained manifest the generator reads, with per-item `id`, `kind`, `state`, `commits`, `pr`, `issues`, `date`, `one_line`, seeded with 13 items (the five recent slices, MERGE-11 to MERGE-16, and two decisions). Its own comments state WHAT BELONGS HERE (machine-readable per-item status) versus WHAT STAYS IN `docs/orchestration/state.md` (the prose narrative, the reasoning, the findings, the authority references, the supersessions), and that the ledger is the record when the two disagree.
- `docs/lessons-learned.md`: the curated AUTHORED lessons source in the repo, eight durable one-or-two-sentence lessons seeded from this project's real ones.
- `scripts/vaultgen/main.go`: the generator, Go, STANDARD LIBRARY ONLY, no new dependency. It reads the manifest, the lessons file, the ADR set and `git rev-parse --short HEAD`, and writes exactly ONE vault file. It validates the restricted YAML so a typo in `kind`/`state`/date fails loudly rather than producing a plausible file, writes atomically through a temp file and rename, and puts NO wall-clock value in the body.
- `scripts/sync-vault.sh`: the wrapper (`--write`, `--check`, `--vault DIR`, default `$HOME/ai-database`), executable, matching the style of the existing `scripts/sync-github-issues.sh`.
- `scripts/vaultgen/main_test.go` and `scripts/vaultgen/testdata/`: the MANDATORY golden fixtures (owner Decision 3). Six tests: the WHOLE generated file against a committed golden (which pins the milestone table including empty-commits and empty-PR rows, the links block, the ADR index reading BOTH frontmatter and the legacy `## Status` format, and multi-line lesson joining); determinism apart from `generated_at`; the hand-edit refusal; `--check` pass-then-fail with the CAUSE named; `--write` refusing a hand-edited file and leaving it byte-for-byte untouched; and the normal create-when-absent path.
The ONE generated vault file, and why that one: `/Users/huy.leminh/ai-database/01 - Projects/assignment-1 (generated).md`, a NEW file BESIDE the authored `Project - assignment-1.md`. It is the design's step-one choice because it REPLACES NOTHING, so the whole step is reversible by deleting the generated note and the generator, and because the authored project note is the single most valuable hand-written artifact in the vault and must not be the first thing a new generator touches.
The stamp, verbatim: a do-not-edit HTML comment naming `source: docs/orchestration/status.yaml`, `source_commit: <sha>`, `date: <YYYY-MM-DD>` and `regenerate: ./scripts/sync-vault.sh --write`; then frontmatter `generated: true`, `generator: scripts/vaultgen`, `source`, `source_commit`, `source_date`, `generated_at` (RFC3339) and `body_sha256` (SHA-256 of everything after the header). `body_sha256` is stable across days and changes only when a source changes.
The four verification checks, all run and all passing: (1) the generated file CARRIES ITS STAMP, header and frontmatter shown verbatim; (2) `--check` PASSES on a freshly generated file, exit 0; (3) `--check` FAILS after a deliberate hand edit, exit 1, and NAMES THE CAUSE as a hand edit (`HAND EDIT: ... body hashes to 69e62355... but the frontmatter declares 99f03822...`); (4) `--write` REFUSES to overwrite the hand-edited file with exit code 1 and the message `REFUSING to overwrite ... it was hand-edited (or its stamp is unreadable). ... Restore it from its source, or delete it, then run --write again.` The generated file was then restored to its correct content and `--check` passes again.
One detail the implementation added beyond the brief, recorded because it is a design choice: the body carries a fifth section, the ADR index, because the design's Answer 2 has the generator read ADR frontmatter and because the mandatory ADR fixture would otherwise be inert; it also reads the LEGACY `## Status` section of ADRs 0001 to 0004 so their status is honest rather than `unknown`. And `--check` excludes `source_date` as well as `generated_at` from the comparison, because excluding only `generated_at` would report a false STALE on any run on a later calendar day.
Archive taken BEFORE the first vault write, per owner Decision 2: `/Users/huy.leminh/backups/assignment-1/2026-10-14/ai-database-pre-records-1-step-one-0937.tar.gz`, 2,356,431 bytes, 94 entries.

The authored `Project - assignment-1.md` is byte-identical to the archive; the dashboard, every daily note, every existing vault ADR, `06 - Knowledge & Wiki` and `99 - Meta` are untouched. No authored note was edited and nothing was archived beyond the pre-write tar.
Verification (parent-run on the frozen snapshot, log `.../T/opencode/records-1-step-one-checks.log`): `gofmt -l` empty on the added Go files; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go vet ./...` exit 0; full uncached `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0 across every package including `internal/keychain`, so the #41 intermittent did not occur; `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...` exit 0; the six vaultgen tests PASS (`TestGenerateGolden`, `TestDeterminismApartFromGeneratedAt`, `TestHandEditRefusal`, `TestCheckPassesThenFails`, `TestWriteRefusesHandEditedFile`, `TestWriteCreatesWhenAbsent`); `git diff --check` clean. Every gate is pass, none is fail or not run, and none was retried. Every pre-existing test still passes.
Operational note carried forward: the stamp names the repo commit the generator was LAST RUN AT, so the vault file becomes STALE relative to the branch tip as soon as another commit lands. That is the intended visible-drift behaviour, and the workflow is to run `--write` after committing.

```text
2026-10-14 / RECORDS-1-STEP-ONE-REVIEW / independent review, fixes, re-review, then merged
Owner authority reference: owner-authorised packet (get a FRESH independent lane on the exact committed snapshot, covering the stamp and hash logic, the refusal path, `--check` false passes, write containment, whether the golden fixtures actually pin the generator, and content leakage; FIX accepted findings and RE-REVIEW; then merge pull request #62 by fast-forward if the review accepts; record that the generated vault artifact was created while the generator was still unmerged). Not authorised: replacing the authored project note, touching the dashboard, answering the design's remaining open questions by implementing them, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any other branch, touching any issue, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification.
Why this review was owed: step one shipped WITHOUT an independent review, and every other slice in this project has had one. This slice adds a generator that will eventually write into the owner's vault, which makes it the least appropriate place to skip it.
Independent review: fresh lane @oracle `ses_ec7b017e3ffeONJNB3hkjFFIaE`, read-only, on `317b439`. Verdict CHANGES REQUIRED. It audited the code ADVERSARIALLY rather than by reading claims, and it CONFIRMED the core safety story: the hand-edit refusal is genuine, byte-preserving and non-zero; `body_sha256` covers EXACTLY the post-frontmatter span including the trailing newline (independently recomputed, with a different digest when the newline is stripped); the body carries no wall-clock value; containment holds for every real path (trailing slash, embedded `..`, spaces in the vault path, and a dangling symlink target all behaved); no leakage (no home path, token, secret or transcript content in the generated file or the program output, and reads confined to the declared sources); standard library only with `go.mod`/`go.sum` unchanged. Its failure battery (no frontmatter, no `body_sha256`, non-hex hash, empty file, truncated stamp, directory, hand edit) all FAILED CLOSED with non-zero exits and left the file unchanged.
Findings and dispositions: F1 (medium) FIXED. `--check` reported a FALSE STALE across a calendar day: the do-not-edit comment's `date:` line is a THIRD wall-clock field and was not excluded, so a file generated on day D reported STALE on day D+1 with no source change. `normalizeForCompare` now blanks all three time fields and does so INSIDE THE HEADER ONLY, which also closes F5 (a BODY line beginning with one of those keys can no longer be masked). A cross-day test now pins it. F2 (low/medium) FIXED. An EMPTY `--vault` was accepted and wrote the file INTO THE REPOSITORY, because `filepath.Join("", rel)` resolves against the working directory, violating the stated invariant that it never writes a repository file. The Go program now rejects an empty or non-absolute `--vault` with a usage error and exit 2, and the wrapper turns an explicit empty value and a bare `--vault` into exit-2 usage errors instead of a silent bare `shift` failure. F3 (low) FIXED. `frontmatterField` scanned the WHOLE header including the comment and returned the first match, so a `body_sha256:` line in the comment SHADOWED the real field and a hand-edited file with a recomputed hash in the comment was OVERWRITTEN (exit 0) instead of refused. It now reads only the block between the two `---` delimiters and requires exactly 64 lowercase hex, treating anything else as an unreadable stamp. F4 (low, coverage) FIXED. The golden did not pin the `|` escaping, the empty-lessons and empty-ADR branches, or the manifest validation; fixtures and tests were added and the golden regenerated. F5 (informational) FIXED as part of F1. F6 (informational, RECORDS ACCURACY) CORRECTED HERE. ```text
2026-10-14 / REVIEW-GATE-1 / the review gate vindicated, recorded plainly
The RECORDS-1 step-one review earned its keep twice, and this is the strongest argument this project has for the review gate. Without an independent review, this repository would have merged a generator that:
  (a) WROTE INTO THE REPOSITORY when `--vault` was empty, because `filepath.Join("", rel)` resolves against the working directory, so a one-argument mistake turned a vault writer into a repo writer; and
  (b) could OVERWRITE A HAND-EDITED FILE, because `frontmatterField` scanned the whole header including the comment and returned the FIRST match, so a `body_sha256:` line placed in the do-not-edit comment SHADOWED the real field and a hand-edited file carrying a matching hash was written over with exit 0.
Both were found by a fresh lane auditing the CODE adversarially rather than reading the session's claims, both were fixed, and both fixes were re-reviewed by the same lane, which verified them by EXERCISING the code. Neither was reachable by the drift check, by the golden tests as they then stood, or by the session's own report. The generator in question will eventually write into the owner's vault, which is precisely why the gate is not optional for a slice that adds a writer. Recorded plainly, as the owner directed, so that the next slice that is tempted to skip the review has the argument in front of it.
Next gate and blockers: none. Production submission remains blocked.
```

- The skill now STATES all three defaults and the exact option labels, in `workflow.md` (a "Guided review defaults (stated, not asked)" section) and in `SKILL.md` (a binding Contract bullet plus the guided paragraph).

The final `Apply this review and mark the day reviewed?` confirmation, the incomplete-source acknowledgement, the carry confirmation and every bulk staging confirmation are byte-identical to before; approval still binds an exact plan, an agent never self-approves, unknown time stays unknown, and no write happens before the deliberate confirmation. The SCRIPTED AND DEFAULT PATHS ARE UNTOUCHED: `cmdRun` is a separate function, the diff is confined to guided-only functions, the machine-readable lines (`plan_id:`, `operations:`, `total:`, `preview blocked:` and the per-operation lines) are produced by untouched code, and the scripted-path tests pass unchanged.
