```text
2026-10-14 / OBSERVATION-SURFACE-1 / authorized -> designed -> implemented -> verified -> reviewed -> review-fixed -> records
Owner authority reference: owner-authorised OBSERVATION-SURFACE-1 packet (covering #48, #49, and #43 if it genuinely closes; offline must be visibly UNOBSERVED and never complete, with a local timestamp never standing in for a remote observation, made true at the type level if the type allows; surface the observation and collision signals where the operator AND the agent will see them, on the scripted path as well as the guided one; if the day's observed entries can now be listed by a supported command, do it and close #43 with the evidence, otherwise state the smallest subcommand and do not build it; no live Gradion access, fakes only; keep the scripted path's machine-readable lines stable or state precisely which changed; full battery, independent review, records, one pull request). Not authorised: merging the pull request, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch, touching issues other than #48/#49/#43 and new ones, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification, vault writes.
THE DEFECT, one thing seen three ways: a remote state that was UNKNOWN was reported as KNOWN. #48 `preview --offline` printed `remote observed: <a LOCAL timestamp>` and `remote complete: true` for a remote it never contacted, and the PLAN recorded the same; #49 the collection-time observation error and collision signals were persisted and tested but printed nowhere, so a failed remote read during `review` passed silently; #43 no supported command enumerated a date's remote entries, so the duplicate guard could not answer the question a past date needs.
DESIGN DECISION 1, offline is UNOBSERVED AT THE TYPE LEVEL, and why that representation. The issue states that `gradion.Listing.Complete bool` cannot express "not observed" and that the honest fix needs a tri-state or a distinct signal RATHER THAN FLIPPING THE FLAG. `gradion.Listing` therefore gains an `Observed bool`, set true ONLY by a successful real read, and the offline branch returns `Observed:false, Complete:false` with a ZERO `ObservedAt`. `submit.observedAt` no longer substitutes a local `now` for an unobserved listing (the `now` fallback survives only for an observed-but-untimestamped listing, which is defensive). `PlanResult` gains a NON-HASHED `RemoteObserved`. So a local timestamp can no longer stand in for a remote observation anywhere, which was the second half of #48 and the half the flag flip would have missed.
DESIGN DECISION 2, surfacing. A shared printer is called by BOTH `review` (scripted) and the guided run's collection section, so the agent, which is the primary consumer, sees the same three signals: the observation-failure warning, the day's observed remote entries, and the collision ids. Nothing is printed for an absent signal, so a clean successful read adds literally nothing.
DESIGN DECISION 3, #43 CLOSES, and the evidence. `review` now performs the day's AUTHORITATIVE collection read and prints the day's observed remote entries with their full ids, spans and descriptions, which IS the enumeration the issue asks for; no new subcommand was built. Evidence: `TestReview_EnumeratesEntriesAndSurfacesCollision` asserts the header and the `  r1  09:00-10:00  already on the remote` line through the real `cmdReview`, and the section is correctly ABSENT offline (`TestReview_UnobservedPrintsNoEntriesSection`). Discoverability was added to both skill files, which is what makes it usable by the agent.
Exact OUTPUT LINES that changed, per mode: OFFLINE `preview` (scripted AND guided) changes `remote observed: <RFC3339>` to `remote observed: unobserved (offline; no remote was contacted)` and `remote complete: true` to `remote complete: false`. ONLINE `preview` is BYTE-IDENTICAL, using the original two print calls. `review` and the guided collection section GAIN up to three conditional lines that were previously absent. The e2e extraction reads only `plan_id:` and runs offline, so it is unaffected.
TWO DISCLOSED CONSEQUENCES, neither hidden. (a) The incomplete-listing gate in `BuildPlan` became `obs.Observed && !obs.Complete` from `!obs.Complete`, because the offline listing is now `Complete:false` and would otherwise BLOCK every offline preview; for a REAL read `Observed` is always true, so the gate is behaviourally identical for real reads, and the reviewer independently verified that the sole real-read implementation sets `Observed` and that no real-read path can wrongly allow an incomplete listing. (b) `RemoteObservedAt` and `RemoteComplete` are HASHED plan inputs, so an OFFLINE plan's `PlanID` changes and an approval bound to an offline plan goes STALE. That is FAIL-SAFE: `Approve` refuses a hash mismatch and `ValidateApproval` blocks with `approval_stale_*`, so nothing proceeds silently and the operator must re-approve. The hash scheme itself is unchanged, and ONLINE plan hashes are unchanged.
Files changed: `internal/gradion/{tools,client}.go`, `internal/run/{submit,collect}.go`, `internal/submit/{plan,reconcile,observer}.go`, `internal/run/run.go` (invariant docs), `cmd/timesheet/{main,interactive}.go`, `.claude/skills/timesheet/{SKILL.md,workflow.md}`, and the test files including the new `cmd/timesheet/observation_surface_test.go`.
Tests: fifteen new or extended, each with fail-before where one exists, including the offline-unobserved pair (run and CLI), the online-observed pair, the plan recording no observation for an unobserved listing, the observed invariant at the read, the review failure warning, the review collision warning, the review enumeration, the absence of the enumeration offline, the guided surfaces, the PARTIAL label, and Reconcile no longer fabricating a local now. Fakes that simulate a real read now set `Observed: true`; ONE existing test asserted the OLD offline behaviour (`remote complete: true`) and was updated, which is stated rather than silent.
Verification (parent-run on the frozen snapshot, log `.../T/opencode/observation-surface-1-final-checks.log`): Verification (parent-run on the frozen snapshot `5d7ecc1`, log `.../T/opencode/observation-surface-1-final-checks.log`): `gofmt -l` empty on the changed Go files; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go vet ./...` exit 0; full uncached `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0 across every package including `internal/keychain`, so the #41 intermittent did not occur; `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...` exit 0; `-race` on `internal/run`, `internal/submit`, `internal/gradion` and `cmd/timesheet` exit 0; the hermetic guided offline harness PASS; every new and changed OBSERVATION-SURFACE-1 test PASS; `git diff --check` clean. Every gate is pass, none is fail or not run, and none was retried. An earlier battery on the pre-fix snapshot `99f66fc`, log `.../T/opencode/observation-surface-1-checks.log`, was also fully green. Focused re-review of the fixes, same lane, on `5d7ecc1`: ACCEPTED, F1 to F4 resolved with no safety-gate change and no new defect.
Independent review: lane @oracle `ses_ec6c02874ffeDfUnWXqgbILEvv`, read-only, on `99f66fc`. Verdict ACCEPTED, and it verified adversarially that NO local timestamp reaches a persisted field offline; that `Observed` is set at exactly one point after a successful builder call so a failed read can never be observed; that the gate change is behaviourally identical for real reads; that the failure warning cannot leak a secret given the typed error contracts; that the enumeration is genuinely the day's authoritative read and absent offline; that the offline-plan approval consequence is fail-safe; and that online output is byte-identical. Findings F1 to F6, all low or informational, dispositioned: F1 (low, the substantive one) FIXED, a PARTIAL observed read was enumerated under a bare header, which made this record's own "never a partial-as-complete set" claim STRONGER THAN THE CODE, so `CollectResult` now carries `RemoteComplete` and the header is marked `[PARTIAL - the read did not complete; this list may be incomplete]`, with the skill wording downgraded to match. F2 FIXED, two `collect.go` comments still said "no CLI surface prints it yet", which this slice made false. F3 FIXED, the observed invariant the plan gate depends on is now documented on `RemoteObserver` and `run.Remote`. F4 FIXED, `Reconcile` no longer fabricates a local `now` for an unobserved listing. F5 (byte-identity is a code-inspection claim, not test-verified) and F6 (the failure warning's secret-safety rests on the Remote implementation's error discipline) CARRIED as informational, with a one-line note added at the print site for F6.
Issue outcomes, with the evidence: #48 CLOSES, because offline is now UNOBSERVED at the type level and the offline plan records a zero observation rather than a local timestamp, verified by the offline-unobserved tests at both the run and the CLI layer. #49 CLOSES, because both signals now have a CLI consumer on the scripted path and the guided one, verified by the failure, collision and enumeration tests. #43 CLOSES, because `review` now enumerates the day's observed remote entries with ids and the skill documents it, verified by the enumeration test through the real `cmdReview`.
Production readiness: unchanged. Production timesheet writes remain blocked, and live MCP, agent and Keychain write verification is still outstanding.
Next gate and blockers: the owner decides whether to merge this pull request. Production submission remains blocked.

Mode 3 is a REGION write INSIDE an authored shared file (the dashboard row) and should be AVOIDED rather than built: the dashboard already uses dataview, so a status row can be DERIVED AT RENDER TIME with ZERO generator writes into an authored file.
2. DAILY-NOTE DRIFT IS NOT FIXED BY GENERATION. It is authoring discipline plus, later, a READ-ONLY BOUNDARY LINTER filed as its own issue; never a generated block inside a note that is hash-identified evidence.

Not authorized: replacing or modifying ANY authored vault file, deleting any vault file, any symlink, any dashboard edit, any change to `state.md`'s format, the daily-note drift mechanism, the per-ADR compacts, the manifest-versus-ledger check, merging the pull request, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification.
What was implemented: `scripts/vaultgen` now generates a SET of THREE vault files instead of one, each with its own stamp (do-not-edit comment plus frontmatter `generated`, `generator`, `source`, `source_commit`, `source_date`, `generated_at`, `body_sha256`) and its own per-file hand-edit refusal. (1) `01 - Projects/assignment-1 (generated).md` keeps its former content MINUS the lessons section. (2) `06 - Knowledge & Wiki/assignment-1 lessons (generated).md` is NEW, generated from `docs/lessons-learned.md`. (3) `01 - Projects/assignment-1 (generated index).md` is NEW and lists each generated file with its path, source, `source_commit` and freshness. `--check` regenerates the whole set in memory and enumerates EACH differing file with an exact cause token (`OK` / `STALE` / `HAND-EDITED` / `UNREADABLE STAMP`), exiting non-zero if any differs. `--write` is ALL-OR-NOTHING ON VALIDATION (every target preflighted for intactness before anything is written, so one hand-edited file refuses the whole set with exit 1 and writes NOTHING) and PER-FILE ATOMIC ON WRITE (temp file plus rename). The index is a pure function of stamp facts: `UNREADABLE STAMP` if the stamp cannot be parsed, `HAND-EDITED` if `body_sha256` does not match the file's own body, `STALE` if the stamp's `source_commit` is not the current commit, else `OK`; a freshly written set does not report the index as differing. The generated body carries NO wall-clock value; `--check` excludes exactly the three time fields (`generated_at`, `source_date`, and the header comment's `date:`) and only inside the header, so no false STALE appears across a calendar day and no body line is masked. Standard library only; `go.mod`/`go.sum` unchanged.
The containment guarantee, stated at EXACTLY the strength the code proves (NOT the packet's absolute wording): containment is decided by FILESYSTEM IDENTITY (`os.SameFile`), with the vault path's longest existing ancestor resolved through `filepath.EvalSymlinks` before an identity walk up to the filesystem root, rather than by a string prefix. COVERED AND TESTED, each a usage error, exit 2, and no write: an empty `--vault`; a relative `--vault`; the repository root itself; a path under the repository root; a `..`-carrying path into the repository; a case-variant path and a case-variant under it (guarded by a case-sensitivity probe); a symlink whose target is the repository root; and a symlink whose target is an inner repository subdirectory, including one with a non-existent leaf beneath it. NOT COVERED, stated rather than implied: a HARDLINK, a BIND MOUNT, and any other mechanism that makes two distinct paths name one file WITHOUT a symlink. The code comment names both lists and claims nothing more; the packet's absolute "no repository path can ever be written" is NOT repeated anywhere, because an overstated guarantee is itself a review-finding class in this repository.

2026-10-14 / GAP-7 / authorized -> implemented (one authorized scope extension) -> reviewed -> records
Owner authority reference: owner-authorized GAP-7 packet for issue #70 (a meeting-derived worklog description is the meeting TITLE, the ICS SUMMARY; cover the meeting block, its carry fragments and the joint block; fix at the MEETING path and NOT in the shared blockDescription helper, and say why; keep a title-empty fallback; decide and record where the ICS body text and the `Location:` line go; disclose the plan-hash consequence in the OBSERVATION-SURFACE-1 shape; tests with fail-before at both layers including the joint block; exclusions; records = this slice plus the carried MERGE-23 record, in ONE pull request that closes #70; then STOP for merge authorization). THE DEFECT: `internal/evidence/plugins/calendar.go:195-196` sets `Title: e.Summary` (the ICS SUMMARY, which IS the meeting title) and `Description: e.Description` with a `Location:` line appended, but `internal/proposal/build.go`'s `blockDescription` returned the Description whenever non-empty and fell back to the Title only when empty, so the meeting TITLE never reached the description the operator reviews or a plan submits; the joint block reached the same wrong text by joining the git group title with the meeting's Description.
What was implemented: a single EXPORTED `proposal.MeetingDescription(e)` selector, title when non-empty else the calendar description text (the required fallback), is now the ONE definition of the meeting rule. It is used at the meeting block (`build.go:367`), its carry fragments (`:381`), and the automatic joint block (`:188`, via `meeting.Description`), and it REPLACED a second Description-first copy in the review layer: `internal/review`'s `ConfirmMeetingsBulk` (`:158`) and `JoinCollision` (`:472`) now call `proposal.MeetingDescription`, and the duplicated `evidenceText` helper was DELETED, so no second copy remains to drift. THE AUTHORIZED SCOPE EXTENSION: the implementer surfaced, and the orchestrator authorized closing, a PARALLEL REVIEW-LAYER path (F1) — `JoinCollision` is reached by an explicit Join decision, which the guided path stages through the "Attended, joined with the overlapping interval (counts once)" option, so before the extension an explicit join still put the ICS DESCRIPTION body into the CONFIRMED joint interval; that was the SAME defect on a REACHABLE route and the packet's exclusion covered only OTHER values, so it was closed here rather than deferred, and the extension is disclosed for owner review.
WHY AT THE MEETING PATH, NOT THE SHARED HELPER: `blockDescription` and `buildManual` are UNCHANGED, because the Description-first preference is CORRECT for every other source: journal evidence sets `Title: item.FirstLine` and `Description: item.Text` where the longer text IS the description, git evidence sets no Description and already falls back to the title, and agent evidence pairs `Title: event` with `Description: topic`. Flipping the shared helper would silently change the journal and agent descriptions. `blockDescription` is still used by `buildManual`, so it is not dead code.
WHERE THE ICS BODY AND THE LOCATION LINE GO, the recorded decision: `calendar.go` is LEFT AS-IS. The ICS DESCRIPTION body and the appended `Location:` line REMAIN ON THE EVIDENCE RECORD, reachable through it, and LEAVE THE SUBMITTED DESCRIPTION whenever the title is non-empty; only a title-less entry falls back to that body. This matches the owner's requirement that they are evidence detail rather than part of the worklog entry we send.
THE DISCLOSED CONSEQUENCE, in the OBSERVATION-SURFACE-1 shape: the block description is APPROVED PLAN CONTENT — `ConfirmProposed` copies it into the `ConfirmedInterval`, `LocalFingerprint` hashes the confirmed interval `Description`, and `PlanHash` hashes operations plus `LocalFingerprint` — so a plan containing a confirmed meeting or joint interval whose ICS body differs from the meeting title gets a DIFFERENT `PlanHash`, and an approval bound to such a plan GOES STALE once that interval is RE-CONFIRMED through the fixed code. The reviewer PRECISED the trigger: staleness comes from the re-confirmation that rewrites the stored `Confirmed[].Description`; a pre-existing record that is NOT re-confirmed keeps its stored body and its approval, so nothing mutates silently. It is FAIL-SAFE: `submit.Approve` recomputes the hash and refuses a mismatch, and `ValidateApproval` blocks with `approval_stale_*` at execute time, so the operator must re-preview and re-approve. Scope is MEETING/JOINT-ONLY; the hash scheme itself is unchanged and no non-meeting plan changes. UNEXERCISED in production, because submission remains blocked and no live run was performed.
Acceptance verification (parent-run on the frozen snapshot `92c7d24`, log `.../T/opencode/gap7-checks.log`): `gofmt -l` empty on the changed Go files; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go vet ./...` exit 0; full uncached `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0 across every package including `internal/keychain`, so the #41 intermittent did not occur; `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...` exit 0; `-race` on `internal/proposal` and `internal/review` exit 0; `git diff --check` clean. Every gate is pass, none is fail or not run, and none was retried.
GATE-HERMETICITY-1, recorded honestly and unchanged: NO gate here is a hermetic claim, because `cmd/timesheet` and `internal/keychain` read the live macOS Keychain. This slice does not change that; the owner has AUTHORIZED a separate slice (GATE-HERMETICITY-1) to fix it, recorded in DECISIONS-6. The reviewer added a useful data point FOR that slice: in this snapshot every test substitutes fakes through the injectable resolver, so the gates it ran did NOT touch the live Keychain — evidence toward the fix, not a hermetic claim for this slice.
Independent review: fresh lane @oracle `ses_ec4fb623bffeHApB0Xc9MMmkNE`, read-only, on `92c7d24`. Verdict ACCEPTED, no blocking finding. It verified title-at-every-meeting-path end to end (meeting block, carry fragments, automatic joint block, `ConfirmMeetingsBulk`, and the reachable `JoinCollision`, whose reachability it traced from the guided join option through `ApplyReview`), swept the WHOLE repository for a surviving Description-first meeting path and found NONE on the approved pipeline, confirmed the empty-title fallback at both layers, confirmed `blockDescription`, `buildManual`, and the journal/git/agent/manual descriptions are unchanged and `calendar.go` is untouched, reproduced the fail-before for all FIVE fail-before tests in a throwaway copy OUTSIDE the repository, and checked the plan-hash disclosure against `LocalFingerprint`, `PlanHash` and `ValidateApproval`. ```text
2026-10-15 / GATE-HERMETICITY-1 / authorized -> implemented -> reviewed -> fixed -> re-reviewed -> records
Owner authority reference: owner-authorized GATE-HERMETICITY-1 slice (no gate command in the standard battery may read the live macOS Keychain; keep the production path behaviourally unchanged with NO default silently falling back to a live lookup; do NOT add a second seam if one exists; DEMONSTRATE the hermeticity with a guard that fails loudly; respect the credential security boundary with no secret in any output, log or fixture; state explicitly what is now hermetic and what is NOT; attempt to reproduce #41 and close it ONLY if the intermittent is genuinely reproduced and explained; records in ONE pull request; then STOP for merge authorization). DECISIONS-6 authorized this slice, superseding the earlier direction to record the non-hermetic gate and leave it unfixed. Not authorized: production behaviour changes, a second seam, any Keychain modification, any secret in output/log/fixture, any vault write, closing #41 without a reproduction, merging the pull request, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch, Gradion access, production timesheet writes.
WHAT WAS FOUND, BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN ASSERTION: the seam ALREADY EXISTED (internal/keychain's `Resolver`, the package var `DefaultResolver` initialized to the real `security`-backed `NewResolver()`, and the test swap `SetDefaultResolver`), so this slice CLOSED A LEAK rather than building a mechanism, exactly as the owner required. A detector was built by prepending a `security` shim to `PATH` that records its argv and `$PWD` and exits 44, then running the battery under it. PRE-FIX the battery made ABOUT 51 LIVE READS, every one `find-generic-password -s gh_auth_token -a huy.leminh -w`, and every one from `cmd/timesheet`, whose 44 `TestGuided_*` and 7 `TestBulk_*` tests resolved the owner's real token through the default resolver; every OTHER package made ZERO live reads. THE FIX, using the EXISTING seam and adding no second one: a `TestMain` in `cmd/timesheet` installs a TRIPWIRE as `keychain.DefaultResolver` whose `Lookup` returns a distinctive, secret-free terminal error at the point of use and counts invocations, failing the whole package LOUDLY after `m.Run()` if the count is non-zero; and the shared guided/bulk runner helper carries a HERMETIC GIT TOKEN through `run.Deps.GitToken`, the documented tests/CI override, which short-circuits git credential resolution before any Keychain call. POST-FIX the same detector records ZERO live reads over both `go test -count=1 ./...` and `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...`, with the whole battery green.
TWO GUARD GAPS FOUND BY THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEW AND CLOSED. F1 (MEDIUM): `internal/run`'s `keychainResolver()` built a FRESH `keychain.NewResolver()` when `deps.Keychain` was nil, BYPASSING the tripwire (the reviewer reproduced a live `calendar_feed_url` read with the counter at zero and the package green); it now returns `keychain.DefaultResolver`, behaviour-identical in production because nothing swaps it there, so that path sits under the existing seam. F2 (LOW): the package-level `keychain.Lookup` silently fell back to `NewResolver()` when `DefaultResolver` was nil; it now FAILS CLOSED with a new secret-free `KEYCHAIN_ERR_NO_RESOLVER` terminal error, satisfying the owner's rule that no default may silently fall back to a live lookup, and changing behaviour ONLY in the unreachable nil state. F3: the guard's wording was calibrated to proven strength. Both fixes carry discriminating fixed-versus-unfixed reproductions.
THE HONEST SCOPE OF THE GUARANTEE, at exactly proven strength: the OBSERVED leak is CLOSED, from about 51 live reads to ZERO, and the two resolving paths in the battery (`keychain.Lookup`/`DefaultResolver`, and `internal/run`'s `keychainResolver` when `deps.Keychain` is nil) are GUARDED, so a live read on either fails loudly. This is NOT a claim that the battery is hermetic against ANY conceivable live Keychain read: a future resolver constructed without `DefaultResolver` would not be covered, and none such exists now. ONE RESIDUAL is recorded rather than fixed (review finding R1): if `DefaultResolver` were set nil AND a Runner were built with `deps.Keychain` nil AND a `keychain:`-ref source enabled, `keychainResolver()` would return nil and the caller would dereference it, a crash on an UNREACHABLE state (production never sets it nil; no committed test does, and the only `SetDefaultResolver(nil)` lives in `internal/keychain`'s own test binary) with NO live read and no security impact; guarding it is available as a one-line follow-up if the owner wants the symmetry. THE OWNER CARRIED R1, NOT FIXED, and required it recorded as a KNOWN LIMIT rather than left implicit: it is a TRACKED CARRY, and the ONE-LINE GUARD is AVAILABLE TO WHICHEVER SLICE NEXT TOUCHES `internal/run/run.go` or `internal/keychain/keychain.go`, so it is a directed hand-off rather than a loose end. Network independence is established by INSPECTION only, and that limit is stated rather than glossed.
ON #41: THE INTERMITTENT WAS NOT REPRODUCED, and the pull request therefore does NOT close #41. The full uncached suite was run six times (6/6 exit 0, `internal/keychain` ok each time), the recorded failing test was run with `-count=150` to completion (exit 0), and the previously-noted concurrency combination (keychain against the heavy co-runners) was run three times (3/3 exit 0). NO CAUSE IS INFERRED FROM TIMING. What this slice DOES establish is that the best candidate on the standard battery is ELIMINATED BY EVIDENCE: `cmd/timesheet` no longer reads the live Keychain (0 detector hits), so the live-Keychain contention path can no longer contribute to a suite stall. #41 STAYS OPEN with its mechanism still unidentified.
CORRECTION TO THE IMPLEMENTER'S WORDING, kept deliberately because an inaccurate claim inside a record is the class this project keeps catching: the implementer reported that under the detector's exit-44 stub `the tests pass` because a not-found credential is tolerated. MY OWN PRE-FIX MEASUREMENT CONTRADICTS THAT: the `cmd/timesheet` guided tests FAILED under the stub with `git_credential [KEYCHAIN_ERR_TIMEOUT]`, so those tests DID depend on a resolvable credential; the stub's exit code and the tripwire's terminal error differ. The record states the MEASURED behaviour rather than the implementer's characterization; the leak and the fix are unaffected either way.
Acceptance verification (parent-run on the frozen snapshots, log `.../T/opencode/gh1-fix-checks.log`): `gofmt -l` empty; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go vet ./...` exit 0; full uncached `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0 across every package including `internal/keychain`; `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...` exit 0; `-race` on `cmd/timesheet`, `internal/keychain` and `internal/run` exit 0; `git diff --check` clean; and the PATH-`security` detector records ZERO live reads over both the unit and the e2e batteries. Every gate is pass, none is fail or not run, and none was retried.
Independent review: fresh lane @oracle `ses_ec17dcae7ffekB72MxDKa9PpDn`, read-only, on `fd5ee4b`. Verdict ACCEPTED, and it FOUND the two guard gaps F1 and F2 above, which were FIXED in `2a01f8d`; the focused re-review on `2a01f8d` returned ACCEPTED, verifying F1 and F2 CLOSED with fixed-versus-unfixed reproductions, NO surviving live path in a full sweep, and F3 addressed, with the residual R1 recorded above. The reviewer also established that the real problem is the live READ itself, independent of pass/fail.

```text
2026-10-15 / JOURNAL-INTERVAL-SUGGESTION-1 / DESIGN PASS ONLY, not implemented (owner-directed)
Owner authority reference: owner-directed DESIGN PASS for issue #33 (read the issue; CLOSE its stated verification limit FIRST by establishing the OBSERVED behaviour of the journal source on a real day, field by field, with the evidence named; use the owner's read-only records under `~/.timesheet/records/workflow/` as the observation surface; do NOT run a live collection or touch the Gradion remote, and STOP and ask if one is genuinely required; answer how a range becomes a suggestion, where it lives, the one-boundary / two-range / no-description cases, and how it avoids DOUBLE COUNTING against manual intervals and on a day that already carries confirmed work; respect ADR-0007's read-only and no-inference contract and the vault standard's own parsing rules; disclose the stale-approval consequence; deliver answers, risks, honest cost and a FIRST REVERSIBLE STEP; NOTHING implemented, and NO pull request for the design; then stop). Not authorized: any implementation, any note write, any live collection or remote access, any Keychain modification, any vault write beyond the granted set, any pull request for this design.
Design verdict (lane @oracle `ses_ec161c263ffeYRAPTtCoLfaSu0`, read-only): THE ISSUE'S VERIFICATION LIMIT IS NOW CLOSED, and the design CONTESTS TWO PREMISES of the input.
THE OBSERVED BEHAVIOUR, and why the earlier attempt could not settle it: `~/.timesheet/records/workflow/2026-10-08.json` shows `.collection.sources` with `journal/journal-work` COMPLETE, count 52, warnings EMPTY, and `collected_evidence` = 86 items = journal 52 + manual 14 + remote_pr 20. The journal source DID run, was complete, and emitted 52 items for a real day: this is the observed collection run the issue said it lacked. A `journal` item carries `id`, `kind`=`journal`, `title` = the bullet's first line WITH the leading `` `HH:MM` `` stamp still inside the TEXT, `description` = the full bullet text, `start`/`end` = the ZERO time, `duration` = `{known:false, minutes:0}`, and `source_refs[0]` = `{kind, at:<zero>, source_id, note_path, heading_path, section, line_start, line_end, stated_date, clock, project_links}` where `section` is `work journal` or `daily impact` and `clock` is EXACTLY ONE `HH:MM` or absent, plus `project_hints` and `observed_at`. WHY THE EARLIER READ SAW ONLY 20 `remote_pr` ITEMS AND ZERO NARRATIVES: the 2026-10-13 backup of that record shows `evidence` = 20 all `remote_pr`, `collected_evidence` = 34, and `.collection.sources` = CALENDAR AND GIT ONLY, so the JOURNAL SOURCE WAS NOT ENABLED for that collection (it WAS enabled for 2026-10-06/07, whose records carry journal items); that is `the source produced nothing because it was not enabled`, NOT a broken plugin and NOT a display artifact, and it self-corrected when a later collection with the journal enabled ran. SEPARATELY, NARRATIVES ARE NEVER PERSISTED AT ALL: there is no narrative field in a record, and `proposal.buildNarratives` RECOMPUTES them from `collected_evidence` at every review, so `the record reports zero narratives` is unobservable from any record whatever the journal produced. The flattened `evidence[]` projection is also NOT the same as `collected_evidence`: it drops `section`, `heading_path`, `description` and the structured `clock`.
TWO CONTESTED PREMISES, corrected by evidence. (a) NARRATIVES ARE NOT ONLY `daily impact` BULLETS: `buildNarratives` has NO heading filter, and 2026-10-08's journal evidence is 33 `work journal` + 19 `daily impact` items, so Work-journal bullets ARE narrative candidates too; the narrow TRUE statement is the INVERSE, that the `daily impact` bullets carry NO clock at all. (b) THERE ARE NO LEADING-RANGE BULLETS ON 2026-10-08: every one of its 33 Work-journal bullets leads with a SINGLE `` `HH:MM` `` stamp, and the only `HH:MM-HH:MM` strings in the note are mid-sentence INCIDENTAL core-hours text (`09:00-12:00`, `13:00-18:00`) plus one inside a SKIPPED section, which the standard explicitly does not treat as a duration. So a suggestion built on this day would produce ZERO intervals: the feature's value on the OWNER'S REAL NOTES is small, and the primary fix is RECORDING DISCIPLINE, not more parsing.
The design decisions, ANSWERED: the boundary data MUST come from the bullet TEXT, because the plugin stores only the FIRST clock and the end boundary is nowhere else; the parse must accept a range that LEADS the bullet (optional marker, optional backticks, `HH:MM` `-`/`–` `HH:MM`) and must NOT scan for a pair anywhere, which is the single most important guard against fabricating work from prose; one boundary only emits NOTHING, per ADR-0007 and the standard's rules 5 and 7; two ranges emit TWO suggestions and are never merged (standard rule 9); a range with no description still yields an interval with an EMPTY description and NO inference; malformed, reversed or equal ranges are rejected. The result is a SUGGESTION, not evidence and not a confirmed interval, so it carries no minutes by itself, and long ranges go through the same composed split as manual and meeting blocks. DOUBLE COUNTING is handled by three EXISTING mechanisms the design studied plus ONE guard to add: `review.Readiness` already blocks ANY overlap between confirmed intervals, an offered duplicate is removed by `user_excluded` while the evidence is retained, and an approval is invalidated on any review-selection change; because 2026-10-08's 14 manual intervals were hand-built from exactly those Work-journal clocks, the ADDED guard is that a suggestion whose span OVERLAPS an already-confirmed interval on the same date must NOT be offered as a confirmable block but surfaced as an advisory `already covered by <id>`, the same shape as the existing remote-collision advisory. On a day that already carries confirmed work the path is ADDITIVE and IDEMPOTENT: suggestions are recomputed from the current note every review, never persisted as confirmed, never written to the note, and keyed so a re-review yields the SAME suggestions rather than new ones, with a changed bullet detaching like any journal item.
THE SHARPEST CONTRACT GAP, a defect either way until it is closed: the vault standard specifies ONE plain range shape (`- 10:33-22:42:`), but the owner's real notes already use shapes it does not cover, including BACKTICKED clocks and a DATE-INTERRUPTED cross-midnight range, so a strict parser would SILENTLY UNDER-COUNT real ranges. The parser and the standard must be made to agree BEFORE any proposal-layer work, and a suggestion that parses differently from the standard is a defect by definition.
RISKS: fabricated work from incidental prose clock text is the TOP risk, the exact outcome ADR-0007 rejected; two DIVERGENT CLOCK READERS (the plugin's first-clock rule versus a new range parser) would drift, so ONE reader is required; double counting with hand-built manual intervals; note-format drift against an underspecified standard; the PRIVACY guard that a suggestion's description (bullet text) must NEVER be forwarded to the external editorial service, exactly as a linked journal description is protected; and a stale approval if the proposal changes without invalidation.
DISCLOSED CONSEQUENCE, in the OBSERVATION-SURFACE-1 shape: a suggested timed block changes what a plan contains, so confirming one changes `PlanHash` and the record's `LocalFingerprint` and an approval bound to the old plan goes STALE. FAIL-SAFE: `ValidateApproval` refuses with `plan_mutated` / `local_changed` and `invalidateApprovalsForDate` marks active approvals invalidated on any review-selection change, and NOTHING auto-executes on a changed proposal.
HONEST COST: moderate to build (a parser, a new proposal channel, review UX, tests, and ADR/standard updates), but ASYMMETRIC, because on the real notes it would produce ZERO suggestions for 2026-10-08 and up to 14 for 2026-10-14 that LARGELY OVERLAP already-confirmed work; the highest-value change is RECORDING DISCIPLINE, not parsing, and the build should be the SMALLEST possible reader for exactly the standard's shape, gated behind the standard first naming the accepted syntax.
FIRST REVERSIBLE STEP: a READ-ONLY, throwaway diagnostic that applies the proposed LEADING-RANGE parser to the five real notes and prints per-note COUNTS and the SHAPE of matched boundaries only (no text, no persistence, no writes), expected to show 2026-10-08 producing ZERO ranges and the incidental core-hours text correctly rejected. Only if that is green: add an optional end-boundary provenance field computed by the SAME rule (no duration change, no block, no suggestion, no confirm, no write; revert is deleting one field), and only then surface suggestions, still non-confirmable by default and overlap-advisory.
CONTESTED AND FOR INDEPENDENT REVIEW, recorded as such: the directing session's `every narrative block is a daily impact bullet` is REFUTED by both code and records; and whether the feature should exist at this cost is a legitimate OWNER question, with the design's own read being to implement only the minimal standard-shaped reader, extend the standard's syntax FIRST, and treat recording discipline as the primary fix.
NOTHING WAS IMPLEMENTED. No file in the repo, the vault, or the records was modified by this design pass; no note was written; no `review` run and no live collection occurred; the remote was never observed. The only side effect was extracting the 2026-10-13 records backup into the approved session temp directory to read the PRIOR on-disk state.
ORCHESTRATION-HAZARD FINDING, added 2026-10-15 by the directing session and recorded because it changes WHAT THE FEATURE IS FOR: the FOUR canonical intervals the diagnostic found in `2026-10-15` are the DIRECTING SESSION'S OWN SESSION-LEG BULLETS (`14:09-14:53`, `14:53-15:11`, `15:11-21:52`, `21:52-22:04`), written under `## Work journal` as that session's activity record, while the OWNER'S REAL WORKING TIME for the day lives in the MANAGED EXPORT BLOCK that the collector deliberately SKIPS. AS DESIGNED, the leading-range parser would therefore convert an AGENT'S ORCHESTRATION LOG into SUGGESTIONS FOR THE OWNER'S WORKING TIME, which the vault standard's OWN NOTE explicitly forbids, that AI-assisted orchestration time is NOT user working time, a distinction written into every daily note's Notes section. This makes the marginal-value conclusion STRONGER, not weaker. THE TWO FIXES, and the ONE THIS RECORD RECOMMENDS: (a) SEPARATE THE TWO KINDS OF BULLET, so orchestration legs live where the parser cannot read them as work, is RECOMMENDED because it is STRUCTURAL and does not depend on a writer remembering a rule; (b) forbid a leading range in an orchestration-leg bullet is the WEAKER, discipline-only fallback. THE OWNER DECIDES. Recorded consequence: at least FOUR of the twenty authorized-scope intervals were agent-orchestration legs rather than owner work, so a corrected count would be SMALLER still.
Next gate and blockers: the OWNER decides whether the direction is right, whether to commission an independent review of the parser contract, and whether to extend the standard's range syntax; NO implementation is authorized by this record.
```

```text
2026-10-15 / DECISIONS-7 / the owner's settled RANGE SHAPES decision for the journal standard
Owner authority reference: OWNER decision, 2026-10-15, the owner's OWN rather than an orchestrator disposition. It settled the syntax gap that the JOURNAL-INTERVAL-SUGGESTION-1 design pass named as its sharpest finding, that the parser and the vault standard must AGREE before any proposal-layer work.
The decision, written by the owner into the vault standard `06 - Knowledge & Wiki/Timesheet time recording standard.md` in a new `## Range shapes` section with `last_reviewed` moved to `2026-10-15`: the CANONICAL shape for NEW bullets is the PLAIN LEADING RANGE, `- 13:00-15:14: finished the review slice`. FOUR LEGACY SHAPES ARE TOLERATED so an existing note is never silently unreadable, each with a real example: backticks around the whole range (`- `10:33-22:42`: session`, 2026-10-12); backticks around each clock (`- `00:00`-`01:00`: ...`, 2026-10-14); a cross-midnight range with a hyphen date interruption (`- 23:27 (2026-10-13)-01:44: ...`, 2026-10-14); and a cross-midnight range with a "to" interruption (`- `22:58` (2026-10-12) to `23:15`: ...`, 2026-10-13). The standard also states what the design decided: only a range that LEADS the bullet is a candidate; a leading SINGLE clock records ONE boundary and nothing more, which is a PARTIAL RECORD rather than an error; and a mid-sentence range is INCIDENTAL text rather than a range. The daily-log template's examples were aligned to the canonical plain form so it stops teaching a tolerated variant.
Consequence recorded: the design pass's FIRST REVERSIBLE STEP, the read-only diagnostic, is AUTHORISED as the ONLY part of #33 authorised for implementation; NOTHING ELSE about #33 is.
Next gate and blockers: none. Production submission remains blocked.
```

2026-10-15 / JOURNAL-INTERVAL-DIAGNOSTIC-1 / READ-ONLY diagnostic, run under the #33 authorisation
Owner authority reference: owner-authorized READ-ONLY diagnostic, the ONLY part of #33 authorised (take the leading-range parser the design produced and run it over the real daily notes that carry workflow records, printing PER-NOTE counts and the boundary SHAPES it found and NOTHING else; no suggestion, no proposal-layer change, no record write beyond the ledger entry, no live collection, no remote access; its purpose is to measure how much real recorded time the SETTLED shape set unlocks, so the decision whether to build suggestions at all rests on a NUMBER rather than an argument; expect 2026-10-08 to yield zero and treat any other result as a finding). TOTAL across the five days: SIXTEEN leading-range intervals, of which FOURTEEN are on one day.
THE MEASUREMENT, which is the whole point: the settled shape set unlocks SIXTEEN recorded intervals across five days, and 2026-10-08 at 77 bullets unlocks ZERO, exactly as the owner expected, so the expectation is MET and the incidental core-hours text is correctly rejected. Of the 16, FOURTEEN are on 2026-10-14, and the design pass already established that those largely OVERLAP already-confirmed work, so the marginal value of building suggestions is SMALL and the case for RECORDING DISCIPLINE over more parsing is now MEASURED rather than argued. NOTHING ELSE WAS DONE: no suggestion, no proposal-layer change, no note or record write beyond this ledger entry, no live collection, no remote access, no Keychain change. The script is throwaway and was not committed.
DIAGNOSTIC SCOPE CORRECTION, 2026-10-15, and the miss is THIS SESSION'S OWN, recorded rather than silently restated: the run above scanned FIVE days because it used the RECORD-DAY LIST SUPPLIED IN THE TASK (2026-10-06, 07, 08, 09, 14) instead of LISTING THE RECORDS DIRECTORY, and that supplied list was STALE. `~/.timesheet/records/workflow/` now ALSO holds `2026-10-15.json`, so 2026-10-15 IS a day that carries a workflow record and belonged in the authorized scope; the instruction to verify rather than trust covered exactly this. SCOPE B, ALL daily notes, wider than the authorization, yields TWENTY-TWO, the extra TWO being 2026-10-12 ONE (backticks-whole) and 2026-10-13 ONE (canonical); BOTH are days that carry a NOTE BUT NO WORKFLOW RECORD and are therefore OUTSIDE the authorized scope. 2026-10-13 answers the completeness question directly: it has NO workflow record at all, so it was never in scope, whereas 2026-10-15 DOES have one and was omitted by this session. THE CORRECTED DECISION INPUT is therefore TWENTY intervals on the authorized scope, still FOURTEEN of them on 2026-10-14, and the conclusion is UNCHANGED: the marginal value of building suggestions is small and RECORDING DISCIPLINE remains the primary fix. AND THE DAY-13 QUESTION IS NOW ANSWERED: 2026-10-13 carries NO workflow record because NO TIMESHEET DAY WAS SUBMITTED FOR THAT DATE. ```text
2026-10-15 / GAP-9-ANTIGRAVITY-ADAPTER / DESIGN PASS ONLY, not implemented (owner-directed)
Owner authority reference: owner-directed DESIGN PASS for issue #73 (read the issue and the metrics adapters; design the antigravity adapter; do NOT implement; PRIVACY NON-NEGOTIABLE, no real transcript committed, copied or referenced anywhere, a SYNTHETIC fixture only; then stop). Not authorized: any implementation, any real-transcript copy, any note or record write beyond the ledger entry, any pull request, any live collection, any remote access, any Keychain change.
TWO OWNER DECISIONS TAKEN ON THIS DESIGN'S RECOMMENDATIONS, recorded here. (1) REFUSE, NOT LABEL, for a forced mismatched provider: a phantom record that increments `RunCount` and later reads as a real cancelled run is WORSE than a refusal because it corrupts the instrument that measures the workflow, and the guard belongs in `Import`. This is the same reasoning that made `keychain.Lookup` fail closed with `KEYCHAIN_ERR_NO_RESOLVER` in the previous slice. (2) NO MODEL CHANGE for the missing cache-write counter: `total_tokens` and `cache_hit_pct` STAY `unknown` for an antigravity import, recorded as a LIMITATION rather than fixed, because emitting a total from an incomplete component set breaks the honesty rule the owner has now kept twice, most recently by refusing an ADR amendment to automate a session-to-day association. THE CONSEQUENCE IS RECORDED PLAINLY: the instrument is of LIMITED VALUE ON THAT HARNESS, which the owner accepts because the owner benchmarks on `pi`, where the adapter is complete.
FACTS CORRECTED BY THE DESIGN'S OWN VERIFICATION, and the ATTRIBUTION the directing session required. (1) The `type` set is `{GENERIC, PLANNER_RESPONSE, USER_INPUT}`; `MODEL` is a `source` value, NOT a `type`. FACT-ATTRIBUTION, one line as directed: that error ORIGINATED IN THE DIRECTING SESSION'S OWN #73 message, which wrote `type` is a semantic role, not a session header: `GENERIC`, `PLANNER_RESPONSE`, `USER_INPUT`, `MODEL`, and which also listed `thinking` among the usage-bearing fields; BOTH ARE WRONG, since `MODEL` is a `source` and `thinking` is thinking TEXT rather than a counter, and the error is recorded against the DIRECTING SESSION'S MESSAGE and NOT against this session's recon or the issue body. (2) `thinking` is a JSON STRING of thinking text, so there is NO reasoning counter and `thinking` must NEVER be decoded. (3) No model identifier exists, so model, price and cost are UNDERIVABLE and stay UNKNOWN. (4) There is NO cache-WRITE counter, which cascades to `ConsumedTokens()` and `CacheHitPercent()` and is why the two owner decisions above were needed. (5) `tool_calls[].args` has no shell `command`, so every antigravity call is UNASSIGNED and a session is UNASSOCIATED without an explicit `--date`. (6) `truncated_fields` names only TEXT fields, never a counter. (7) The issue cites ADR-0002, which ADR-0005 SUPERSEDED on 2026-10-09. (8) `.tickets/M3-02:26` carries a stale `antigravity-cli` path; the real path is `~/.gemini/antigravity/brain/...`. (9) A `transcript_full.jsonl` variant exists with the same shape minus `truncated_fields`. (10) The silent-empty-record path requires `--date`; without a derivable date `Import` already fails closed.
THE DESIGN. Detection: a five-scalar header discriminator (`step_index` numeric, `created_at` non-empty, `source` in {MODEL, USER_EXPLICIT}, `type` in the semantic-role set, `sessionId` absent), registered in `SelectAdapter` as `--provider antigravity` AND in the `auto` slice; the three adapters are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE by construction, so order is immaterial. Provenance OBSERVED, verified first-hand, contrasted with claude's declared-only, so the report's `[declared schema]` marker is NOT printed. Decode ONLY counters and the timestamp, plus a `timesheet` subcommand token if a `command` field ever exists; NEVER decode `content`, `thinking`, tool-call names or args (including a filesystem path and prose), `status` or `error`. `truncated_fields` gates counter TRUST: the line is COUNTED, since its counters are intact, but any counter the marker names is downgraded to UNKNOWN. Skill guidance replaces the pi-only `$PI_SESSION` with the conversation-id-derived path under `~/.gemini/antigravity/brain/<id>/.system_generated/logs/`. The invented-shape benchmark fixture is NOT mutated, because it pins the LEGACY extractor that ADR-0006 leaves unchanged; a NEW synthetic metrics fixture is added and the legacy one is recorded as a misnamed asset with a bounded follow-up. NO OBSERVATION-SURFACE-1 CONSEQUENCE: `internal/metrics` is a non-blocking accounting surface with no coupling to `PlanHash` or approvals, so nothing there needs disclosing.
TWO POINTS CONTESTED AGAINST THE ISSUE'S FRAMING, carried to the #69 design pass: `records the counters` is only PARTLY true, because the three COMPONENTS are recorded but `total_tokens` and `cache_hit_pct` stay UNKNOWN; and the counters do NOT flow into a day's lane, because all calls are UNASSIGNED and a session is UNASSOCIATED without `--date`. #69's benchmark rewiring presumes the adapters cover the harnesses, so both points are folded into that design pass.
PRIVACY: the real transcript was inspected read-only for FIELD NAMES, TYPES and SET MEMBERSHIP only; no value and no message content was printed; the conversation id `6ea37403-eb9a-49aa-8b03-66b49a0bc6ef` appears in the issue and this record only, and NOTHING ELSE from that conversation was copied, committed or referenced anywhere.
NOTHING WAS IMPLEMENTED: no file modified in the repo, no branch, no commit, no pull request, no Keychain access, no live service.
Next gate and blockers: the OWNER decides whether to build the adapter, whether to rename the legacy fixture, and (already answered above) REFUSE and NO MODEL CHANGE. No implementation is authorized by this record. Production submission remains blocked.
```

2026-10-16 / DEFECT-2-CLASSIFICATION-IDENTITY / authorized -> implemented -> reviewed -> fixed -> re-reviewed -> records
Owner authority reference: the queue handed over at the orchestrator resume, whose FIRST item is issue #31: "the `resolveEditFromObservation` post-edit projection omitting `ClassificationID`, carried as F6 from INCIDENT-REPAIR-1, which needs its impact on reconciliation and mapping refresh established before it is fixed". Session authority: owner-delegated full driving authority plus the five standing instructions in `control-policy.md`, which permits selecting slices, writing packets, dispatching lanes, committing on an authorized branch and opening pull requests, with every merge reserved for separate owner authorization. Not authorized: force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch or remote ref, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification, and any vault write beyond the narrow per-merge grant the owner states in that merge's packet.
THE REQUIRED IMPACT QUESTION WAS ANSWERED FIRST, AND THE ANSWER IS A NEGATIVE THAT THE RECORD STATES RATHER THAN BURIES: IMPACT ON RECONCILIATION IS NIL, AND IMPACT ON MAPPING REFRESH IS NIL. `Reconcile` (`internal/submit/reconcile.go:134`) compares only a persisted baseline against a fresh observation, through `RemoteContentEqual`, and the post-edit projection is a function-local that is never persisted, so the omission cannot change a match kind, a `remote_conflict:<localID>`, or any unresolved reason. Mapping refresh is unaffected for the same reason: `refreshMappingLastObserved`, `ApplyReconcile` and the execute-time `BuildMapping` source classification from the observed listing entry or from the plan, never from the projection. What remains is DECISION-LEVEL, and it is where the defect is real.
THE DEFECT: `resolveEditFromObservation` (`internal/submit/resolve.go:188`) built `postEdit := prior.LastObserved` at `:203-207` and overwrote ONLY Date, StartTime, EndTime and Description from the outcome row `o`, so `postEdit.ClassificationID` stayed the PRE-EDIT classification while `RemoteContentEqual` (`internal/submit/approval.go:127-138`) DOES compare it, at `:134`. The root cause was that `workflow.OperationOutcome` carried no classification field at all, because `gradion.BuildOutcome` (`internal/gradion/outcome.go:109-118`) copied those four fields from the plan and dropped the plan's classification.
TWO HARMS, both decision-level, both now covered by regression tests: (i) FALSE NEGATIVE, a classification edit that DID apply but whose outcome was unresolved could never confirm, because both `resolve.go:210` and `:218` fail when the projection carries the old classification and the observation the new one, so `RestartGate` blocked the day indefinitely; (ii) FALSE CONFIRM, the sharper case, a CLASSIFICATION-ONLY edit that did NOT apply compared EQUAL against the unchanged pre-edit baseline, so it was recorded `OutcomeConfirmed` while the correct `GRADION_WRITE_CONTENT_MISMATCH` guard had ALREADY FIRED at `execute.go:358`. The pre-fix red output proves the second one exactly: `Status:confirmed` on a row still bearing `ErrorCode:GRADION_WRITE_CONTENT_MISMATCH`.
What was implemented: an ADDITIVE `ClassificationID string` field on `OperationOutcome`, tagged `omitempty`, stamped in `gradion.BuildOutcome` from `planned.ClassificationID` and on the in_flight INTENT row in `submit/execute.go:163-175` from `op.ClassificationID`, so a crash before the terminal outcome leaves a classification-aware row rather than a blind one, and projected in the resolver ONLY when non-empty, so a legacy on-disk row keeps today's behaviour exactly. NO `SchemaVersion` bump. `RemoteContentEqual`, `plan.go`'s `desiredRemoteState`, `DeriveOperationKey`, `PlanHash`, `LocalFingerprint`, `BuildMapping` and the `RemoteState` struct were NOT touched, and classification was deliberately kept OUT of the operation key.
Acceptance verification, PARENT-RUN on the frozen snapshot before the commit (log `.../T/opencode/defect2-checks.log`): `gofmt -l` empty on the changed files; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go vet ./...` exit 0; full uncached `go test -count=1 ./...` exit 0 across EVERY package, TWENTY-SIX `ok` lines including `internal/keychain`, so the #41 intermittent did NOT occur; `go test -tags e2e -count=1 ./tests/...` exit 0; `go test -race -count=1 ./internal/submit/... ./internal/gradion/...` exit 0; `git diff --check` clean. Every gate is pass, none is fail or not run, and none was retried.
Independent review: fresh lane @oracle `ses_ebfc19fc4ffe8bhzYFQZpO5IQt`, read-only, on the UNCOMMITTED snapshot whose flattened diff hash was `9e7e13fccd58b27cd13763a0d233affbb7b32a7cd9234f0f5b1897023f47e8d4`. Verdict ACCEPTED, NO PRODUCTION DEFECT FOUND. It verified the additive claim INDEPENDENTLY rather than by trusting the new test: it compiled a harness in a throwaway copy OUTSIDE the repository against BOTH the base and the fixed `models.go` and obtained BYTE-IDENTICAL marshalling for a legacy row, an in_flight row, a whole `Record`, `LocalFingerprint` and `PlanHash`. It swept ALL SIXTEEN `OperationOutcome` consumers and found none affected, having checked specifically for a map keyed by the struct, whole-struct equality, `reflect.DeepEqual`, outcome-keyed dedupe, a golden fixture pinning the ledger's key set, and any document enumerating ledger fields. It REPRODUCED the discriminator itself, rebuilding the pre-fix wiring in its own copy and diffing its red output against the implementer's, identical apart from timings, and it judged the "only when non-empty" guard the CONSERVATIVE choice, noting that the unconditional alternative would read a legacy "unknown" as "empty" and create a NEW false confirm.
FINDINGS AND THEIR FIX ROUND, five test-quality findings and no production defect. ACCEPTED AND FIXED in a second round whose flattened diff hash was `23dd93e06bf4c86d8b89a095b5ae75a15f9d4be409ca6840fb076ed2bef1cbec`: INT-1 a VACUOUS assertion, a `PlanHash` comparison that hashed the same unmutated plan twice and therefore COULD NOT FAIL, so it defended nothing; it was DELETED rather than replaced by a fake, with the meaningful `LocalFingerprint` half kept and the structural reason recorded as a comment instead of pretended as a test. INT-2 the reviewer established that calling the new marshalling test a "pin" was OVERSTATED, because against the pristine pre-fix tree the package does not compile and the test therefore cannot literally pass before and after; the label was corrected to a specification of the additive contract. INT-3 unguarded `rec.Outcomes[0]` indexing in both new sub-cases, which would PANIC rather than fail cleanly, now bounds-guarded with a clear fatal message. DECLINED after the reviewer called them adequate: the inert resolver argument in the execute test and the whole-string `classification_id` substring check, both left as written rather than churned. FOCUSED RE-REVIEW by the same lane on the fixed snapshot: ACCEPTED, with coverage NOT weakened because the deleted assertion covered nothing and `PlanHash` retains dedicated coverage elsewhere, NO FALSE PASS possible from a test-only round, and the new guards shown STRICTLY STRONGER than what they replaced because they can only add failures.
FOUR PRE-EXISTING ITEMS FILED RATHER THAN ABSORBED, with a dedupe pass FIRST, and ONE OF THEM CORRECTED BEFORE FILING because the reviewer showed the claim as first stated was WRONG: #81 a second same-key edit is dispatched with NEITHER a durable intent NOR an outcome row, which the reviewer found the implementer had UNDERSTATED, so a crash after that write leaves NO ledger trace at all, and which also BOUNDS harm (ii) to the first edit per key. #82 the mapping's top-level `ClassificationID` is NOT refreshed on the inference-confirm and reconcile paths and IS written from the LOCAL interval choice on the adopt path, the blanket "never updated" wording being WRONG, with the bounded impact recorded as a stale fingerprint input rather than a wrong verdict. #83 `markNotApplied` overwrites the originating failure code, so the durable row loses the mismatch evidence even though the run result keeps it. #84 the same vacuous-assertion idiom surviving in `internal/workflow/editorial_persistence_test.go`, found in a file this slice does not touch.
TWO BOUNDED CARRIES, recorded rather than left implicit. First, the LEGACY ON-DISK RESIDUAL: a row written before this fix carries no classification and therefore keeps today's behaviour, so both harms survive FOR THAT POPULATION ONLY. The reviewer proved the residual BOUNDED, because no new row can be created with an empty classification since `resolveClassification` blocks an unclassified plan, and it is FAIL-SAFE on the applied side and unreachable-from-new-rows on the unapplied side. Confirming that no existing record holds such a row before production enablement is the OWNER'S call, since it needs the private operator records. Second, the LIVE QUESTION of whether the remote listing echoes `classification_id` in the form the operator chose, which needs authorized read-only live access that this session does NOT have, and which is therefore NOT VERIFIED; the fix is fail-safe if it does not, in that a correctly applied edit would stay unresolved rather than falsely confirm.
THE RE-CUT OF THIS SLICE'S BRANCH, recorded here because this is where the narrow owner grant was exercised, with ALL FOUR conditions verified and stated rather than assumed: `fix/defect-2-classification-identity` had ZERO commits ahead of `main` (it sat two records commits BEHIND it); it had NEVER been pushed and had NO upstream, shown by `git branch -vv` and by `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/fix/defect-2-classification-identity` returning nothing; the uncommitted work lived in the slice's own worktree, which the owner required to be verified rather than assumed, and it was: after the move the same NINE files remained modified, the flattened diff hash was UNCHANGED at `23dd93e0...`, and every per-file hash was identical. 2026-10-20 / GAP-6-VENDOR-BUNDLE / authorized -> implemented -> reviewed -> fixed -> re-reviewed -> LOST -> recovered byte-exactly -> records
Owner authority reference: the queue handed over at the orchestrator resume, whose second item is issue #58, "the tracked vendor bundle documenting the messaging surface rather than the timesheet tool contract". Session authority: owner-delegated full driving authority plus the five standing instructions in `control-policy.md`. Not authorized by this slice: any code change, any `.gitignore` change, any tracking of the bundle, any file under `references/`, merging the pull request, force-push, history rewrite, reset, merge commits, deleting any branch or remote ref, Gradion access, production timesheet writes, Keychain modification.
THE QUEUE'S OWN WORDING IS WRONG AND THE SLICE CORRECTS IT RATHER THAN REPEATING IT: THE BUNDLE IS NOT TRACKED AND NEVER HAS BEEN. `.gitignore` lines 11-12 read `# references/ is a vendor bundle deliberately kept out of the repository.` and `references/`; `git ls-files references` is EMPTY; `git check-ignore -v` resolves to `.gitignore:12`; and `git log --diff-filter=A -- references/` is empty over the whole history, so nothing under `references/` has ever been in the repository. Issue #58's phrase "the bundle now tracked at ..." is therefore incorrect, and a note placed inside `references/` would have been ignored. The slice's note is a TRACKED file under `docs/`, and it states the true status: a deliberately untracked local copy, not reviewable through the repository and not a source of record.
What was delivered: `docs/gradion-vendor-bundle.md`, NEW, 101 lines, recording what the bundle is (a skill bundle for the Gradion Workspace MESSAGING product, naming its five files), the corrected git status above, a "What it is not" section (it carries no timesheet tool schema and points at the catalog at `GET /api/me/apps/mcp` and `tools[].inputSchema`, so a schema claim must come from a live read), TWO HIGH cautions, seven LATENCIES with the first two deferred to the owner rather than decided, a scope close, and provenance. Plus `docs/gradion-mcp-verification.md` section 10, `+16/-0`, appending a vendor-bundle boundary without renumbering or altering any earlier section.
The TWO HIGH CAUTIONS, each verified against the bundle rather than assumed: (a) TRANSPORT SURFACE, the bundle documents REST for the messaging product plus a REST app-proxy and NEVER mentions native MCP JSON-RPC at `/api/mcp`; this was verified as genuine silence and not merely absence of a string, since `jsonrpc`, `initialize` and `tools/list` are absent too, while MCP `tools/call` and `inputSchema` appear only as REST-proxy shapes. (b) EXAMPLE MISMATCH, the bundle's lone timesheet-flavored TOOL example is `log_hours` with `{project, hours}` against `/api/me/apps/timesheet/tools/log_hours/call`, which does not match our live-verified `timesheet__log_time` field set.
Independent review: fresh-in-context lane @oracle `ses_ebfced242ffe7IqvFhGMtj9W5y`, read-only, on the uncommitted snapshot identified by `docs/gradion-vendor-bundle.md` SHA-256 `5557fb44634d1ea11c95d84dec5424642825ef020db21367d39d67bf46bae243` and `docs/gradion-mcp-verification.md` SHA-256 `e918894f247a668879cd9ce53c6f45fdc541a029bacd3653ffae9ec3b5e43c1e`. ROUND ONE returned CHANGES REQUIRED on F1, and it was right: the label at `docs/gradion-mcp-verification.md:287` read "HIGH, illustrative example", which RE-INTRODUCED in the OTHER file exactly the attribution this slice exists to remove, contradicting the note's own negative claim. F2 (the deeplink bullet juxtaposed two different surfaces) and F3 (a "defers" intent gloss) and F4 ("lone timesheet-flavored example" narrowed to "tool example") were ACCEPTED and fixed. F5 and F7 were declined as no-action. ROUND TWO, on the fixed content, returned ACCEPTED AS-IS, having re-verified each corrected passage against the source, confirmed the change is confined to the two paths, confirmed the seven discrepancies remain LATENT with none asserted as decided, and confirmed section 10 appends without renumbering.
THE OVER-LITERAL CHECK WAS MINE AND WAS CORRECTED RATHER THAN KEPT, recorded because the wrong direction is easy to miss: I required a zero-match grep for `illustrat`, and the writer dutifully reworded the note's NEGATION to "a mere sketch" to satisfy it. That was cargo-culting a check: the requirement was never that the string be absent, but that no POSITIVE attribution be made. The precise negative was ordered restored, and the reviewer confirmed the permitted state explicitly: EXACTLY ONE occurrence of `illustrative` in either file, inside the negation "does not itself mark the example as illustrative".
THE ATTRIBUTION OF THE UNSUPPORTED CLAIM, corrected on issue #58 itself rather than only here: the claim "the example is explicitly illustrative" originated in THE LIBRARIAN-1 RECORD and in ISSUE #58's body, travelled into THIS SESSION'S OWN FIX PACKET, and was copied faithfully by the writer. The writer copied its packet correctly; the error was the packet's. A comment was posted on #58 stating this, and a separate correction entry in this ledger supersedes the LIBRARIAN-1 wording without rewriting it.
THE LOSS, RECORDED PLAINLY BECAUSE IT HAPPENED: the slice's worktree was created under the operating system's TEMPORARY directory, and FOUR DAYS of wall-clock time passed while this session waited on an owner merge authorization. The operating system cleaned that directory. The work had NEVER been committed and NEVER been pushed, so at that moment both delivered files existed ONLY as bytes that no longer existed on disk; a filesystem search for `gradion-vendor-bundle.md` across the temp trees, `/tmp` and the projects directory returned NOTHING. The earlier orchestrator had already reported on 2026-10-16 that "nothing has landed in four days; you were waiting on me, not on a race", which is exactly the condition that made the wait harmless to the merge and harmful to an uncommitted temp worktree.
THE RECOVERY, AND WHY IT IS VERIFIABLE RATHER THAN PLAUSIBLE: the SAME lane that wrote the files was resumed, and it reproduced both from its own context. Both SHA-256 values MATCHED THE RECORDED VALUES ON THE FIRST ATTEMPT, and THIS SESSION VERIFIED THEM ITSELF with `shasum -a 256` rather than accepting the lane's report: `5557fb44...` and `e918894f...`, byte-for-byte. Because the acceptance had covered those EXACT bytes, identified by hash before the loss, no new review was required and none was claimed: the recovered content IS the reviewed content, proven by hash rather than asserted. Had either hash failed to match, the plan was to STOP and report rather than commit a near-equivalent.
THE OPERATIONAL LESSON, recorded so it is not repeated: a WORKTREE UNDER THE SYSTEM TEMPORARY DIRECTORY IS NOT DURABLE ACROSS A MULTI-DAY WAIT. Slice work must either be COMMITTED PROMPTLY, so the ref protects it, or be MATERIALIZED UNDER A DURABLE PATH. The loss cost nothing in the end only because the exact bytes had been recorded as hashes as evidence for review, which is the same discipline that let the recovery be proven instead of hoped for.
THE RE-CUT OF THIS SLICE'S BRANCH, under the narrow owner grant, with ALL FOUR CONDITIONS verified and stated: `docs/gap-6-vendor-bundle` had ZERO commits ahead of `main`, sitting FOUR commits behind it; it had NEVER been pushed and had NO upstream, shown by `git branch -vv` and by `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/docs/gap-6-vendor-bundle` returning nothing; the uncommitted work lived in the working tree and was identified by the two hashes above BEFORE the move and IDENTICAL AFTER it, verified rather than assumed; and the operation was a REF MOVE ONLY, from `20e3f03` to the then-current `main` `e64737a`, immediately before this commit. No pushed ref was touched and nothing was rewritten.
THE REVIEW COVERED A PRE-COMMIT SNAPSHOT, stated rather than glossed, and in this slice the hash discipline proved its worth twice over: the acceptance covers content identified by hash, the recovery reproduced that content exactly, and the commit therefore carries exactly the reviewed bytes. Owner authority reference: the driving handoff of 2026-10-20 delegates FULL DRIVING AUTHORITY with the five standing instructions and names #33 as the incoming direction's first decision. The JOURNAL-INTERVAL-DIAGNOSTIC-1 record left the final gate to "the OWNER decides whether to build suggestions at all, on this number"; under the standing delegation this session takes that gate on the same number and records it as an ORCHESTRATOR DISPOSITION the owner may reverse, following the attribution rule established by the GAP-7 authority-attribution correction: delegated calls are stated as this session's own and never written up as owner decisions.
THE DECISION: #33 IS NOT BUILT. The minimal standard-shaped reader is not commissioned, and issue #33 is CLOSED AS NOT PLANNED with label `wontfix` and a comment citing this record. The measured number the call rests on is stated first, so the call can be attacked rather than its reasons absorbed.
THE MEASURED BASIS (JOURNAL-INTERVAL-DIAGNOSTIC-1; the authorized scope is the notes whose days carry a workflow record): TWENTY leading-range intervals across the six scope days as measured, FOURTEEN of them on 2026-10-14, which by the design pass largely OVERLAP the day's ALREADY-CONFIRMED work because those fourteen manual intervals were hand-built from exactly these clocks, and ZERO on 2026-10-08, whose seventy-seven bullets lead with single clocks. FOUR of the twenty are 2026-10-15's agent-orchestration legs, excluded from work time by the ORCHESTRATION-HAZARD finding and the settled bullet split. THE REFRESH the new directing session RAN rather than carrying the number: through 2026-10-20 the authorized-scope count is UNCHANGED AT TWENTY — 2026-10-16 now carries a workflow record and its note yields ZERO leading-range and zero leading-single-clock bullets; 2026-10-19's note carries ONE canonical owner-work range `10:00-11:00`, but that day carries NO workflow record, so it is OUTSIDE the authorized scope; 2026-10-20's one range bullet sits under `## Agent session log` and is therefore an agent leg, not owner work. NOTHING since the diagnostic has unlocked one additional owner-work interval inside the scope.
WHY THE OWN BEST CASE IS A ROUNDING EFFECT, rather than a feature: the recording discipline is already DELIVERED and IS the primary fix. The owner's DECISIONS-7 placed the canonical leading range and the four tolerated legacy shapes INTO the standard, so notes are now written in exactly the syntax a future reader would accept, standardized WITHOUT being parsed; the daily template teaches the canonical form; and the bullet split keeps agent legs out of work time. Across seven now-scope-known record-carrying days the reader would produce ZERO intervals on five, agent-leg advisories on one, and overlap advisories on the fourteen duplicates of the last.
THE PROPORTIONALITY TEST, on the design's own honest cost: moderate to build (a parser, a new proposal channel, review UX, tests, ADR and standard updates) against a measured product of overlap advisories on known duplicates and nothing on most days. The queue behind it is non-speculative maintenance with known defects: `#81` to `#84`, `#86`, `#72`, the plans `#21`, `#24`, `#29`, `#85`, and the `#69` design with the two `#73` points folded in. Building ahead of those bar spends the window improving the parsing of a rarity while known defects wait.
THE REOPEN CONDITION, so the closure carries its own evidence test rather than a mood: #33 is REOPENED with the specific day attached IF the owner records a work-annotated day whose `## Work journal` carries canonical leading-range bullets NOT already covered by confirmed intervals submitted through the existing manual/review path. One such day changes the number this decision rests on, and that is what reopening a number looks like.
THE HONEST PRICE OF NOT BUILDING: untimed items stay untimed in the collector, which is what the issue's own title states, single leading clocks record one boundary for good, and NOTHING in this disposition changes the collector's emission behaviour. The acceptance is that review and recording discipline cover, at a measured TWENTY intervals of which the duplicate-existent path already reaches no new one, what the reader would have covered.
WHAT THIS RECORD DOES NOT DO: it authorizes NOTHING else in the queue, closes nothing but #33, and does not pre-empt the OWNER'S OWN pending decision on `#56` mode 2, where the owner loses something and the record must not be spent at the end of a long session.
Issue closure method: #33 is handled through the tracker by the executing session on this packet, closing as NOT PLANNED with label `wontfix` and a comment citing this record; the tracker account is the owner's, so the record carries the true actor and authority here.

## Documentation review

On 2026-10-10 an independent documentation reviewer identified seven gaps. Corrected actor labels for orchestrator-only commits, explicit session vault authority, shared-workflow coverage, provisional remote limit/timezone assumptions, mapping ownership, review snapshot/independence fields, and significant-outcome daily logging. Corrections affect documentation only; S1 source review remains pending. Independent re-review of these documentation corrections has not been run.
